## Main question reached

The precise Q in Engel's Conjecture 8.2 cannot be the limit of the untwisted family in any proper flat holomorphic filling with smooth nearby fibre. Its virtual Euler number is zero, independently of any elliptic-fibration map. The direct plumbing constructs the nodal alternative with Euler number one; two minimal crepant subdivisions construct smooth-total-space semistable alternatives with a specified blow-up and small resolution as normalization. Their nearby fibres are the actual untwisted X(u).

For the S6 family, the finite-place semistable construction exists, but the proposed global assembly is inconsistent. Its central cycle would define a degree-12 component cover over the three-punctured base. The finite monodromies rotate by +4 and −3; the plain cusp has identity permutation. Their product cannot be identity, even after reversing either local labelling. A different cusp model is therefore necessary.

The necessary component curve has the explicit model b³=a⁴+1. Its pulled-back elliptic surface is Y²=X³−3bX+2a². Three explicit sections give the full integral Mordell–Weil group; an integral Néron–Severi determinant and component-height congruences prove that its twelve-fold deck trace image is exactly 12ZP. Thus no section has trace6P, excluding the split ruled-normalization repair. The full NS determinant is−32. Boundary_d independently checked the geometric and lattice trace proof.

The nonsplit alternative can be taken further: the nonzero class6P in the cyclic cohomology quotient has an everywhere-unramified invariant genus-one torsor preimage. Hochschild–Serre gives a generic preimage; local norm calculations and Ogg–Shafarevich localization remove its ramification. Its projective genus-one surface has an affine deck liftA satisfyingA¹²=t6P. The normal line exists and has square−2, and an integral automorphism of the genus-three Jacobian gives M/(A*M)=O(infinity). The resulting compact auxiliary plumbing has twelve nodes; after small resolution its smooth Euler number is24 and its cusp multiplicity is12. Thus this concrete construction supplies a comparison model with precisely quantified defects, not a filling of the actual sphere family.

The remaining toroidal scope has also been enlarged. A.2.4 exhaustively parametrizes every finite-orbit invariant smooth refinement by bounded primitive rays and finite determinant, common-face, volume and boundary checks. Each passing certificate gives a proper compact modification, including nonminimal top refinements and mixed cusp rays. The normalization charts and every central multiple locus are explicit. A.4.8 determines all integral homology groups: writing k for pure-top ray orbits, m for mixed ray orbits and t4 for maximal-cone orbits, the free Betti tuple is (1,1,8+k+m,7,9k+t4−2m−1,0,k+m). A.2.5 supplies a terminating blow-up/down algorithm and refutes the literal blow-up-of-R shape. A.4.9 supplies the second integral normalization-stratification computation for every input fan.

## Geometry, topology and independent checks

R has Betti numbers (1,0,11,0,11,0,1); each modified normalization R' has (1,0,12,0,12,0,1). Both are simply connected. Q has free homology with Betti numbers (1,1,8,7,10,0,1), fundamental group Z and Euler number 12. Each minimal corrected central fibre has free homology with Betti numbers (1,1,9,7,11,0,1), fundamental group Z and Euler number 14. Their dual complex is a circle.

The untwisted X(u) has free homology with Betti numbers (1,1,8,14,8,1,1). For the positive u-circle, N=T−I acts only on H3, has square zero and rank six. Its nonzero Smith entries are 1,1,1,1,1,11. The calculation uses the unimodular rational-elliptic-surface lattice, the primitive classes F, P−O and 6P−O−3F, and the Thom/Gysin/Mayer–Vietoris sequences of the constructed semistable model. The relevant rank-two negative lattice has determinant 11.

The D agent independently reviewed the analytic virtual Euler argument, branch K-class, compact core/node construction, normalization blow-up charts, component-cover obstruction, split trace proof, unramified torsor proof and the new unrestricted fan/gluing construction in A.2.4. D also opened the original AKMW and Matsuki factorization theorems and passed the full A.4.8 proof, including finite analytic scope, every centre type, hidden-stabilizer exclusion, noncompact blow-up splitting and the reference normalization maps. The E agent independently checked the integral A.4 normalization and monodromy computation, and the root agent reviewed its ranks and discriminant. These are independent AI checks, not human refereeing. The analytic proof cites Levy's perfect-complex comparison and Łojasiewicz's compatible triangulation; the algebraic Fantechi–Göttsche theorem is not used as an unproved analytic extension.

R8's line bundle and inverse lift, R10's local equation/pinching, and R1/R11's finite-action formulas were checked at use. Finite covariance identities were recomputed from the matrices.

## Corrections to supplied material

Lead 1 confuses the pointwise Euler function with its integral on C*. Lead 3's uniqueness needs the minimal/joint-flat toroidal restriction and is false even for joint-flat families if arbitrary horizontal blow-ups are allowed. Lead 4 confuses a torsion specialization with a generically non-torsion line bundle. The second finite component shift is −3 in the fixed orientation. Lead 5 omits a nonextendable overlap; component permutations already contradict its gluing. R2's H1 vanishing is not a theorem about extension across r=0.

The contracted cusp diagonal has direction e1−e2. It differs from the e2-direction curve fixed by Aut(C*). The base-changed minimal cusp fan has n+1 bridge choices, not only the two endpoint base changes.

## Unfinished work and approaches attempted

The main missing step is a cusp degeneration compatible with the finite component shifts and the actual two-parameter translation cocycle. The naive product and plain-cusp gluing are refuted. The corrected finite root models and untwisted fans exist, but none has the required cusp component rotation.

A polarized multivariable Mumford theorem cannot simply be applied because the period units need not satisfy its symmetry hypothesis. Direct compact plumbing proves the untwisted case instead. R2's fixed-parameter cohomology vanishing supplies no boundary control for the overlap exponential involving log(s)log(r). The degree-12 cyclic component cover with ramification (3,4,12) exists as a curve, but does not construct a central threefold or smoothing. Schröer–Siebert's Hopf-normalization degenerations smooth to primary Kodaira surfaces with positive Chern class; that feature prevents a direct application to these torus fibres. Schnell's analytic Néron comparison assumes polarizability, and Kamenova's result assumes a Kähler total space. Neither proves intrinsicness of the component torsor in the present analytic setting.

The cyclic cusp-root fractional-period repair fails because a nonzero r-component shift forces nonreduced q-fibres, whereas the given general family has reduced Mumford fibres. The alternative split ruled normalization fails because trace6P is absent from the explicitly computed section group over the necessary genus-three cover. A nontrivial genus-one torsor with precisely that affine descent obstruction exists by B.3.6, even with trivial local torsor classes. Tsen's theorem does not assert that a genus-one torsor has a point. B.3.7 now supplies its normal line M, with M²=-2 and M/(A*M)=O(infinity), and proves the compact auxiliary plumbing. The auxiliary smooth fibres have Euler characteristic24, and their cusp has multiplicity12. These two defects are calculated, not conjectured. The twelve node charts are not identified by the affine map A: their centres belong to neither plumbing collar. A proper birational modification over B cannot remove the multiple cusp, since every cusp valuation is divisible by12. A different cusp construction with a verified punctured-family comparison remains necessary.

B.4.2 proves without a Kähler assumption that the nearby fundamental group surjects onto that of any reduced normal-crossings centre with smooth total space. The proof is given both by Nakayama–Ogus logarithmic rounding and by a local gradient retraction followed by the transverse-meridian argument. Thus any actual S6 centre must be simply connected. Every bare pinching of one pair of disjoint conductor sections maps onto the circle on fundamental groups, so no such pinching can be the required centre, regardless of its elliptic or torsor normalization base. The parent's separately sourced B.4.3 is included immediately afterward and supplies additional coherent/Hodge-cohomology obstructions; it does not assert that all possible analytic cusp components belong to the prohibited classes.

The separately saved A.4.7 gives a uniform result for every toroidal output: if k is its number of pure top ray orbits and t4 its number of maximal cones in a fundamental prism, its central Euler number is11k+t4 and its fundamental group isZ. Its nearby Euler function integrates to2, with the two contributions forced by the fixed bottom triangles and determinant1. A.4.8 now determines the full free integral homology of every output by reference normalization maps and toroidal weak factorization. Its proof explicitly permits temporary pure-q blow-ups and uses only the finite cone-complex theorem, realized by analytic monomial ideals; it does not assume an unstated general relative analytic factorization theorem. A.4.9 now completes the second computation by central proper pushforward and the explicit ordered-branch/projective-subspace matrices. The root agent independently refereed A.2.5 and A.4.9. The separate B.3.8 proves b1=6 and Kählerness for every analytic locally trivial torsor under the genus-three Jacobian, even nonalgebraic and nontorsion ones. Its ruled bundles and compact smooth bimeromorphic models therefore still satisfy the ddbar lemma. These supplements were saved separately to keep the main TeX stable during the parent's build.

The mu6 action, fixed loci, quotient resolution, least base-change exponent, global sphere-limit central homology and the intersection numbers needed for Eells–Kuiper remain unproved. No values are assigned. A next construction must give a non-ddbar cusp normalization carrying the required component rotation, prove that its punctured restriction is the actual S6 family, and exhibit its finite-place overlap maps before properness or topology is asserted. The present arguments do not prove that every possible analytic cusp component is ruled or toric.

## Detailed record: B_hodge_report.md

## B.4.3: a necessary non-Kähler central stratum

The new obstruction applies to every positive base-change exponent, not just the proposed exponent six. A proper semistable filling of the actual six-sphere family would have a simply connected central fibre D, by B.4.2. On the other hand, the independently checked root-curve direct images give h¹(X,O)=1, and upper semicontinuity gives h¹(D,O_D)≥1. Thus H¹(D;C)→H¹(D,O_D) cannot be surjective.

Graf and Kovács (2014, §1.D, p.120) state surjectivity for a proper algebraic variety with Du Bois singularities, including normal-crossings singularities. Consequently D cannot be such an algebraic variety. For a simple normal-crossings D, Chen (2026, Definition 2.3 and Theorem 2.4(4)) shows that the ddbar lemma on every closed stratum forces E1 degeneration of the ordinary Hodge–de Rham sequence on a nearby fibre. That contradicts b₁(S⁶)=0 and h¹(X,O)=1. In particular, a construction using only compact Kähler or Moishezon components cannot work in this category.

The independent lower bound h¹(O)≥1 for any complex structure on S⁶ was also proved: analytic Riemann–Roch gives χ(O)=0, while Stokes' theorem kills holomorphic top forms and Serre duality gives h³(O)=0. Hence h¹(O)=1+h²(O). The small exact program b_hodge_obstruction.py checks the weighted-section count used in the more specific family calculation; the mathematical argument is in the paper, not delegated to that program.

The primary Chen version 2 of 16 October 2026 was opened and its additional K1 hypothesis was checked at the exact point where parts (3) and (4) of Theorem 2.4 are stated. K2 and a local invariant-cycle theorem are not assumed. The original Graf–Kovács journal PDF was opened. An independent agent read both primary statements and checked the proof; its full mathematical referee record is E_B_hodge_referee.md.

This does not show that an unrestricted analytic filling cannot exist. An arbitrary three-dimensional component over the cusp has not been proved Moishezon. B.4.3 originally gave its analytic ddbar conclusion for simple normal crossings; B.4.5 below now proves the stronger normalization-component obstruction for all normal crossings by a direct argument. The algebraic obstruction also includes normal crossings with self-intersections, because the local Du Bois condition applies there.

Further primary reading tested the missing implication. Nishiguchi's 1988 preprint *Degeneration of K3 surfaces, II*, Theorem 1.4 and Example 2.1, describes analytic semistable K3 degenerations with Hopf components. Istrati, Otiman, Pontecorvo, and Ruggiero (2022, Theorem 7.1) construct proper flat toric-Kato degenerations with smooth total space. These are different families. They show why non-Kähler quotient geometry deserves examination; neither supplies a comparison map or a filling for the family here. No such application is asserted.

The next unresolved step is concrete: either construct an analytic cusp model whose non-Moishezon stratum satisfies the prescribed punctured-family gluing, or prove that every possible filling is forced into a class excluded by B.4.3. Properness alone does not establish the latter.

## B.4.4: extending the marked base map

A possible essential projective-coordinate ambiguity has now been removed. For an arbitrary proposed semistable filling, the coherent direct image of the relative bi-anticanonical line bundle is torsion-free of rank two on the disc. A frame gives a meromorphic pencil. Its proper graph has an analytic critical-value image. The three intrinsically distinguishable punctured critical values extend as degree-one branches of a finite analytic curve. Their cross-ratio normalization therefore has meromorphic coefficients, even if the values collide at the centre. Composing gives the extension of the exact marked map f to P¹.

This is a meromorphic map and a projective graph modification, with the categories and comparison on the punctured disc specified. We do not assume that the graph is smooth or semistable. The construction uses only the particular coherent direct-image and base-change statements already opened and checked in E.3; the local graph and its critical locus are explicitly constructed. A second independent reader checked the source in E_B44_referee.md.

The reduction does not compare the actual punctured family with the auxiliary genus-one-torsor family, whose Euler number is 24, and does not show that an arbitrary vertical component is Moishezon. Thus it advances the unrestricted filling question without closing B.4.1.

## B.4.5: the self-crossing case and normalization components

The simple-crossing restriction has now been removed for the component obstruction. For any compact analytic normal-crossings D, including self-crossings, ddbar on its normalization components forces H¹(D;C)→H¹(D,O_D) to be surjective. The proof uses the ordered distinct-local-branch resolution and its alternating summand. A coherent degree-one class is represented by a (0,1)-form α on the normalization and a function f on the branch-pair spaces. The ddbar lemma replaces α by a d-closed representative; the cocycle equations then make f pluriharmonic on compact branch-pair components, hence constant. The same pair is consequently a de Rham cocycle lifting the class.

The distinction between local branches and global components is essential: the construction retains two branches of a self-intersection of one irreducible component. No ddbar assumption on intersections is needed in degree one. The local analytic resolutions are justified by convergent monomial expansions, with permutation-equivariance and simplex exactness checked by b_nc_degree_one.py. The proof itself does not depend on a finite computation proving the analytic step. The independent referee is E_B43_degree_one_referee.md.

Combining with B.4.2–B.4.3 shows that every semistable centre for this family, after any positive finite base change, must have a normalization component failing ddbar. In particular no model with only Kähler or Moishezon normalized components can be the desired limit. This settles the proposed extension of the Hodge obstruction to self-crossing toroidal models; it does not force unknown analytic vertical components into the excluded class, so the unrestricted existence and least-exponent question remains open.

## Detailed record: C_push_report.md

## C: actual smooth monodromy zero by two determinations; analytic filling open

The actual positive-w-loop monodromy is proved to be zero in Theta7=Z/28, with the complex fibre orientation and the generator of Eells-Kuiper value 1/28 fixed in C.1. Its oriented mapping torus is S1 times S6. C.3.24 and C.3.27 each prove the original one-loop result, not merely the vanishing of its sixth power. Their boundary-invariant conversions and integer-level one-loop arguments are independent; they share the actual annular representative and its geometric Pontryagin and cubic inputs. C.4.1 extends the zero to the other admissible sphere twist data; the antiholomorphic transformation law remains the one already proved in C.4.2.

The mapping-torus connected-sum comparison is proved with the positive-loop convention and its boundary orientation. Both spin structures give the same Eells–Kuiper value. A concrete positive generator has Euler class 1, p1=6, p1²=36, signature 1 and mu=1/28.

Lead 6's spin-c coefficients are correct when the boundary determinant trivialization induces the spin structure used in the comparison. The formula is derived by eliminating p2 between the spin-c Dirac index and the signature theorem, and checked against Crowley–Nordström's normalization in exact symbolic arithmetic. H² through H⁵ of the mapping torus vanish; the p1 lift is unique rationally, the c1 lift need not be unique, and the displayed numbers are independent of that ambiguity.

The circle action and the local shear can be verified, but a global scalar-gauge isotopy is invalid: the primitive gamma coordinate on a regular torus cannot extend to the simply connected sphere. Furthermore all 28 sphere mapping classes possess representatives for a semifree circle action with fixed S². Thus that equivariance alone cannot decide the residue. Sixfold base change leaves an explicit order-two ambiguity {0,14}.

The class is unchanged by the admissible sphere twist data via the parameter-holomorphic comparison maps in E.1.3. The antiholomorphic involution reverses both fibre orientation and loop direction; it imposes no order-two constraint.

### First determination: the equivariant orbit-knot integer

C.3.10 identifies the actual six-loop return with a lift of the marked annular gamma translation. C.3.14 supplies its actual spin six-dimensional Wall surgery cap. C.3.16 computes the actual cusp-link row (12,0,1), and C.3.17 gives a marked product cusp quotient by an explicit deformation and small-resolution calculation. C.3.18 constructs an actual embedded genus-one Lefschetz four-manifold with twelve positive nodes bounding the marked three-torus, and computes its affine stable-frame Pontryagin defect -24. C.3.19 transports that defect through the full derivative clutch and actual Euler class; local unipotence alone is not used to discard the global frame.

C.3.20 expresses the cap cubic as epsilon_cap times three times the actual delta-line relative square. C.3.21 identifies the entire mixed Picard class of that line with the original normal-function section, using the exact multipliers, nodal excision and integral multiplicative Leray. Its actual square is -2. The explicit Jacobian comparator has twelve I1 fibres and chi(O)=1; no characteristic number of the auxiliary compact-core filling is transferred. C.3.22 calibrates the boundary-frame versus coordinate-clutch signs directly by connections and gives the common formula J=epsilon_cap*(-24-12*(-2))/24=0 in Z.

C.3.23 defines the additive integer of equivariant clutching before reduction modulo28. The actual closings compose coherently, all transport changes are equivariant, and C.3.24 therefore has 6*q(Phi)=q(Phi^6)=-J=0 in Z. It follows that q(Phi)=0 and hence [Phi]=0. This is the extra argument resolving the order-two ambiguity; one cannot divide 6[Phi]=0 inside Z/28.

### Second determination: an actual spin-c coboundary and direct index

C.3.25 constructs an actual eight-dimensional coboundary of the clutched sphere. Start with the disc bundle of the meridian line over the actual six-dimensional surgery cap and attach one product four-handle along the Hopf S3 times D4 piece. Its boundary replaces that free piece by the actual fixed D4 times S3 tube. The Euler class and a logarithmic boundary gauge identify its remaining free exterior with the actual circle bundle. Thus the boundary comparison is part of the construction.

On the integral fourth-homology basis consisting of PD(x) and the added CP2, the intersection form is [[k,1],[1,0]]. The added CP2 has its explicit product-framed normal D4. The p1 covector is (p+k,3) and the determinant-square covector is (k,1). Consequently the signature is zero and the three relative characteristic pairings are p1²=6p-3k, p1*c²=p+k and c⁴=k. Boundary connected sum with S1 times D7 gives an actual mapping-torus coboundary with the same numbers. The spin-c formula gives mu=(p-4k)/672, with no Wall or Davis conversion. At the actual six-loop values p=-24*epsilon_cap and k=-6*epsilon_cap, its pairings are -126*epsilon_cap, -30*epsilon_cap and -6*epsilon_cap, and their spin-c combination is zero.

C.3.26 independently defines the integer lambda=(p-4k)/24 by the twisted spin Dirac index. Its additivity follows from actual characteristic bordisms: the Y-bundle over a pair of pants gives mapping-torus additivity, and the Y-bundle over an annulus with two N times I pieces attached over opposite outer arcs compares those mapping tori with the actual caps. Both the tangent bundle and the meridian line extend, so p1*x and x³ are individually additive for fixed-tube-relative maps. A change of fixed normal framing contributes the sphere bundle S(eta) over S4. Its vertical Euler class 2x gives p1=4x², so lambda is unchanged. This argument uses no three-knot integer or its group law.

C.3.27 uses the same exact equivariant six-loop comparison, then 6*lambda(Phi)=lambda(Phi^6)=0 in Z. It follows that lambda(Phi)=0. The explicit eight-dimensional coboundary now gives mu(Phi)=lambda(Phi)/28=0, which detects the zero class by C.1.2. This is a second determination with independent conversion and one-loop arguments. The two methods share the actual p,k geometry; they are not calculations from disjoint geometric data. Independent agent referees and separate characteristic terms are not being counted as additional methods.

### Scope still unfinished

The specifically requested almost-complex eight-manifold W of B.4 has not been constructed. Its central-fibre intersection form, c1,c2,p1, relative lifts, p1²,p1*c1²,c1⁴ and signature have not been computed. The explicit smooth spin-c coboundary of C.3.25 is a different construction. Its determinant class c is not asserted to be the first Chern class of an almost-complex tangent bundle. The verified formula and the two actual boundary computations do not assign Chern numbers to the unconstructed analytic filling. This exact remaining task is Open in C.5.1.

### Verification and failed approaches

The proof was checked at the actual geometric comparisons: root and D refereed the annular reduction and cap; root and E refereed D's actual cusp product, page and frame defect; root and D refereed E's cubic transgression; root, A/B and D independently refereed the actual Picard/Leray square; root, D and E calibrated the signed cap formula; root and D refereed the equivariant integer and first one-loop bridge. Root and E accepted the explicit eight-dimensional construction; root and D accepted the direct index additivity and second one-loop bridge. The latter checks are recorded in E_C325_C326_referee.md and D's separate referee. Exact programs audit the lattice matrices, wedge signs, Jacobian equation/discriminant, section, cover genus, eight-dimensional characteristic arithmetic and direct-index framing invariance. They do not replace the geometric proofs.

The audit in E_C_attempts_report.md retains the false scalar-gauge shortcut, the disproved literal rank-three quotient, the nonbounding local torus, auxiliary Euler24 models, and the temporary sign mismatch. The latter was corrected by distinguishing the inverse transitions for a boundary frame and a coordinate clutch, independently of the expected cancellation. No novelty claim is made.

The additional primary-literature audit identifies the second construction as established Hopf surgery/index machinery. Jiang (2014), proof of Lemma3.5, Case2, gives the same line-disc-bundle/Hopf-handle construction with the product-normal CP2 and the k=0 spin-c evaluation; his Lemma3.6 displays (p-4k)/24. C.3.25 retains general k and verifies the particular actual boundary here. The author-hosted April2014 manuscript was opened and visually checked, and publisher metadata was verified separately. Montgomery and Yang's original 1968 introduction, pages125–126, was also opened through the publisher preview for the historical action/knot/projective-space correspondence. The reading scope, the distinction from the earlier arXiv version, and source-access failures are recorded in E_C_index_sources.json and E_C_index_search_log.json. Full source PDFs remain outside the deliverable.

Sources opened: Eells–Kuiper (1962), §3 and §6, formula (11), bundle calculations (12)–(13); Kervaire–Milnor (1963), primary pages 504–505, Theorem 1.1, the table and Lemma 2.1, plus the bP8 order calculation; Poénaru (1971), Theorem 1 and Corollary 1, page156; Crowley–Nordström (2020), Lemma 2.1 and equation (2.1); Durán–Rigas (2007 preprint / 2009 publication), Theorem 2.1 and §3.2. The original Cerf1970 scan was initially too large for web extraction but subsequently downloaded successfully; its primary page8, Théorème0 and Corollaires1–2, was read and visually inspected. The Γ4=0 book is bibliographically verified but not opened; it is not substituted for the dimension-six theorem. The primary downloads, metadata-only review output, and rendering commands are recorded in code/c_fetch_primary.py, code/c_review_primary.py and code/c_render_primary.py and their outputs. The scans and private text extracts are not republished.

## Sources read and exact inherited steps checked

Read the complete supplied prompt. Read the return's AB.tex A.1.1 canonical evaluation calculation and A.4.1 full translation-lattice Albanese computation; CD.tex C.1–C.3, including the local theta-polydisc calculation. The canonical evaluation gives h0(K^-1)=1 and divisor 2S2. The Albanese lattice requires the half-period translations, not merely the fixed eigenspace. The local calculation supplies an analytic coordinate h=mu-(1-tau)/2, a nonzero derivative of e2, and log N=log sqrt2-(pi/2)|h|²+O(|h|⁴); the coefficients needed below have been rederived.

Opened Buchdahl (1999), Theorem 11, pp. 296–297, from the original journal PDF; Grauert (1960), §6 Hauptsatz I and §7 Sätze 1–2 (the last is not used after the erratum check), from the original journal PDF; Cornulier's author PDF, Theorem 3.1 and its proof, pp. 10–11. The final direct-image argument in D.1 proves base change over the smooth base curve by the long exact cohomology sequence and the closed discrete torsion support of R1π_*L; it does not invoke the originally stated generic base-change theorem.

## Corrections

An initial draft cited Grauert (1960), §7 Satz 2 for a closed analytic base-change exceptional set. The foundations audit found that Grauert's 1963 erratum corrects precisely this assertion. The final proof removes this citation: coherence alone plus 0→L --t→L→i_*L_b→0 yields the exact canonical base-change sequence, and the torsion of the coherent R1π_*L on a smooth curve has closed discrete support. The mathematical theorem remains supported by the repaired argument.

Lead 8's outline becomes a proof only after removing the proper images of the nonsmooth loci, then the base-change exceptional set, then the reduced anticanonical divisor's singular and critical loci. Each exclusion is closed discrete in its then-current open curve; the final complement is at most countable, sufficient to leave a boundary-circle point in an open good locus. A global closed-discrete-union assertion is unnecessary and has been removed. The latter reduction avoids treating the nonreduced divisor 2S2 as a smooth surface. The fibrewise-isomorphic variant needs no family isomorphism or Kuranishi calculation.

Lead 9's first-order period direction is correct but its conclusion is false. For a=0, h=1/n and the integral vector n²(gamma+3u−3w)−6n w+3n delta, the period tends to (eta,xi)=(0,−3i). This is outside the asserted moving complex line modulo the central period group. The overlooked scale is n²: the quadratic term survives after the linear moving period has been cancelled. Exact model arithmetic is in code/d_push_exact.py. The actual analytic period differs by O(n^-2), proved in D.2.1. The central fibre's own Hausdorffization, the leaf-space Hausdorffization and the Hausdorffization of the entire root family are three different objects.

## Computation record

`d_push_exact.py` uses exact SymPy arithmetic to check the transformed period determinant, the entire dual-lattice formula, the cancellation responsible for the quadratic degeneration, the counterexample lattice vector, the theta-derivative constants and the rational central-circle period. Output is `d_push_exact.out`. An initial expected determinant sign was wrong; the failed assertion and correction are preserved in `d_push_exact_initial_error.out`. No theorem rests on floating-point arithmetic. `d_push_numeric.py` evaluates the equianharmonic crossing height in 110-digit mpmath arithmetic by two distinct expressions (theta series divided by eta, and the cancelled theta/eta product); both give 1.848362329764999227374945515886084283435696822219612834750098356396585750079055814438, differing by 6.6530623e-111. Equality with the proposed closed form was not inferred from these numerical comparisons; it is now proved independently by D.4.5. The two methods share arbitrary-precision arithmetic but do not share the expression being summed.

## Literature search record

The initial proof identified the zero set with the modular-norm level in D.4.2, but superharmonicity and connected sublevel sets did not exclude additional maxima. This gap was recorded as Open in D.4.6 and retained through the earlier checkpoint. The equianharmonic height was first supported numerically and then proved analytically in D.4.5. The correct Grauert erratum URL https://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__35_0.pdf was opened and its item 2 on printed p. 36 read: the non-bijectivity locus in original §7 Satz2 need not be closed. This confirms the repair recorded above. Cornulier's publisher PDF https://msp.org/agt/2011/11-4/agt-v11-n4-p06-p.pdf was opened; Theorem3.1 remains numbered 3.1 and its proof is on pp.2017–2018. Bibliographic details: Algebraic & Geometric Topology 11(4), 2007–2035 (2011), DOI 10.2140/agt.2011.11.2007.

## Additional global-level-set approach

The exact modulus derivative is ∂τD=iZ(3Zwp′−2g2)/(4π²wp′), where Z=∂μlogθ1+2πi Imμ/Imτ. The heat-equation derivation is D.4.4. `d_push_gradient.py` checks it against an independent symmetric difference of the product expression at three moduli in 80-digit arithmetic; discrepancies are below 9e-25 at step 1e-12. This check is supporting evidence, not a critical-point certificate. The resulting precise task was determining zeros of both factors away from elliptic ramification; it is now certified below. Searches for "Weierstrass zeros Green function theta norm critical points modular function" and related terms led to Lin–Wang (2010), whose Theorem1.3 and introduction were opened. That result counts critical points in the elliptic-curve variable at fixed modulus, whereas our problem differentiates in modulus; it therefore cannot replace the needed factor analysis. URL: https://www.math.purdue.edu/~eremenko/Pdf/Lin/lin-annals.pdf .


## Exact equianharmonic crossing completed

D.4.5 proves N((2−rho)/3,rho)=3^(1/4) and hence Dpart=−(3/(2π))log48. The proof first determines the zero mean of log N directly from the horizontal average of the Jacobi product. Multiplication by alpha=1+rho has matrix [[1,−1],[1,2]], degree3, and kernel {0,±mu}. The functions log N(alpha z) and the sum of log N(z+v) over this kernel have identical distributional Laplacians. Their difference is harmonic and its mean is zero, so it is identically zero. Taking z→0 gives N(mu)^2=|alpha|=sqrt3. The R3 normalization then supplies the exact crossing height. This is a complete analytic argument, not a numerical conjecture or an assumed closed form for beta. The lattice, mean integral and logarithm coefficients are checked in code/d_push_equianharmonic.py/.out. At that stage the remaining open statement was renumbered D.4.6; its subsequent resolution is explicit. The final Part-D count is17 rows:16 in D_push_results.csv and D.4.7 in its separately maintained CSV. No novelty or priority is claimed.

## Supplemental independent reviews and the closing cocycle

The parent requested independent review of the virtual Euler obstruction and of the mapping-torus orientation. These checks are saved separately in sections/D_referee_AB_C.md, with two primary references in sections/D_referee_references.bib and exact checks in code/d_push_referee.py/.out. Both A.3.1 and A.3.2 validate; the analytic specialization proof was sharpened using the codimension-three singular set in the inverse image of a radial segment. The review also checked the global core/node plumbing, the two normalization modifications and the degree-12 component-cover obstruction for literal Lead5.

The investigation of the proposed monodromy order bound produced an explicit affine lift of R2 and a source comparison with the known v1 lift. Its durable output is the fully proved Proposition C.3.5 in sections/C_closing_cocycle.tex, with a separate registry row. The exact scalar t^4/(t−1)^3 has finite orders4,−3 and cusp order−1; its actual cusp map also has a two-coordinate holomorphic unit representative. The latter distinction prevents the scalar pole from being treated as an intrinsic obstruction to an arbitrary quotient isotopy. At that initial stage, patching the scalar and cusp-unit isotopies was unproved and no order bound was asserted. Subsequent joint work resolved the actual annular reduction, its characteristic cap and the one-loop passage: the final numerical conclusions are C.3.24 and the independent index/coboundary determination C.3.27. The earlier open note is historical, not the current status of Part C. Both the earlier lift and the clean known normalization were checked in code/d_push_monodromy.py/.out and code/d_push_closing.py/.out. The audit trail and corrected intermediate gamma coefficient are retained in sections/D_monodromy_order_note.md.


## Coarse regularity at the exact crossing

D.4.5 also now proves that the coarse function is C¹ at t=0, with Dpart(t)=Dpart(0)+(7/(32π))Re(t)+O(|t|^(4/3)) and Wirtinger derivative tending to7/(64π). The norm is invariant under rho², which fixes mu modulo the lattice, so Z vanishes there. The heat equation gives Z_tau=iP/(4π), Z_bartau=0; Ramanujan gives g2'=3iP²/π. Substitution in D.4.4 and division by t_tau from the cubic modular ramification gives the exact coefficient. Thus the zero level at H0 is a regular C¹ arc tangent to the imaginary axis, not a singular coarse level. The complex modulus derivative vanishes only because t has order-three ramification. The exact coefficient arithmetic was added to d_push_equianharmonic.py. The separate d_push_equianharmonic_crossing.py uses90-digit arithmetic and compares the theta derivative with a finite difference of the product norm at three nearby moduli; discrepancies are below1.2e-34. This is a supporting implementation check, not a numerical proof.

## Completed global critical-factor certificate and level classification

The exploratory `d_push_global_scan.py` evaluates19,922 points with complex128 arithmetic and36 Fourier terms, including499 samples of the circular modular boundary. Its largest wp residual is below3.0e-13; it records358 unsuccessful root-solving starts and is explicitly not a proof. It found no unexpected factor zeros, which motivated the rigorous calculation.

The compact calculation `d_push_global_arb.py` uses the independently certified E.4 root enclosures for the19,701 exact rational rectangles. It independently sums the derivative factors through36 Fourier terms with explicit geometric-moment tail bounds and outward Arb arithmetic at192 bits. Its first complete pass proves18,263 rectangles, assigns1,425 whole closed rectangles to |tau−i|<3/20000, and leaves13 coarse rectangles near rho undecided. The original run and its failures remain in the deliverable. `d_push_global_refine.py` replaces those13 rectangles by67 exact dyadic child rectangles using18 subdivision nodes at depth at most2. It redoes74 strict root contractions, proves56 refined leaves and assigns11 whole leaves to |tau−rho|<1/50. The final cover therefore has18,319 nonvanishing leaves,1,425 i-leaves and11 rho-leaves. The smallest proved absolute lower bounds exceed0.00133357953708 for Z and13.58699524669 for F=3ZP−2g2. The paper uses the weaker exact rational bounds13/10000 and13.

`d_push_global_cover_audit.py` verifies the exact original cover, every subdivision, every local-disc containment, the input hashes, the positivity bounds and the polynomial identity det_R Pi=Im(tau)Im(beta)−6(Im(mu))². Its PASS result is saved as JSON and ordinary output. The original cover has36 omitted rectangles inside the i-disc and174 rectangles strictly outside the modular domain, and its exact area is23/40. Every boundary point is covered; there is no numerical gap between the finite cover and local proofs.

The independent root contribution D.4.7, in sections/D_global_local.tex and code/d_root_gradient_certificate.py, supplies the i-disc, rho-disc and infinite cusp region. Its local-i error is less than0.198862|h| against the leading coefficient pi/2. For every Im(tau)≥2 the q-disc contraction and independent interval0≤1/Im(tau)≤1/2 give |Z|>1.39894 and |F|>127.018. Near rho,400 exact offset rectangles prove derivative closeness to the nonzero complex-linear leading terms. The failed one-square image-inclusion test was preserved and was not called a contraction. Its derivative norm nevertheless is below0.432523 on the fixed root square of radius1/80, giving uniqueness there. The independently checked400 refined root boxes all lie within coordinate distance0.007730605<1/80 of the same centre, so the local analytic root branches glue. These independent checks are code/d_push_rho_branch_audit.py/.out and d_push_rho_containment.py/.out.

The resulting real function f=−Dpart is a proper C1 submersion from the finite plane minus{1} to(H*,infinity). To avoid using a smooth-fibration theorem at a merely C1 point, D.4.8 constructs a smooth vector field with df(V)>0 on each compact height band. Its smooth flow has unique C1 hitting times for each level; this transports the known small Jordan curve to every finite supercritical height. Thus every level is one C1 Jordan curve, real analytic except possibly at the unique regular t=0 crossing. Its interior contains1 and has D>0; its exterior contains the cusp and has D<0. The interiors are nested and exhaust the plane. This closes the earlier Open item D.4.6 rather than leaving the computational evidence as a conjecture.

The additional primary-source search and exact theorem comparisons are in sections/D_global_literature_audit.md. Chen–Kuo–Lin2019 AppendixA verifies the classical derivative identities independently; the fixed-torus and generic-degeneracy theorems of Chen–Fu–Lin2025 do not replace this composite modulus calculation. All added bibliography entries are marked opened. No priority is claimed.

## Scope and attribution

This section is AI-produced by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode). It claims no priority and attributes no result to a person beyond the statements actually read in the cited papers. Its assigned E.1, E.2, E.3 and E.5 items are resolved, with the configuration correction explicitly marked Refuted. It is a contribution to the full S6_02_PUSH package, not a complete answer to the whole prompt. E.4 belongs to the root agent and is included in its correct position immediately before E.5. Completion of this section does not change any remaining status in Parts A–D.

## Main deductions and corrections

The integral map from the prompt's marking to Viaclovsky's logarithmic-transformation marking is

`PV = [[-2,1,0,1],[-6,0,-1,0],[0,-1,0,0],[-1,0,0,0]]`, with determinant −1.

It sends the fixed vectors to `-u0` and `delta-u1`. Inverting the clockwise generator gives `w1=N0 epsilon-rho0 delta_hat` and `w2=N1 epsilon'-(rho1+N1)delta_hat`. The `N1` correction in the second formula is essential. The reference section has zero cusp-comparison class. This fills the dictionary gap identified in R5 and supplies actual root-disc, middle and cusp maps for sufficiency. For standard sphere twists the parameter is `W=(-1)^s2 w`; the comparisons vary holomorphically locally, and their connected automorphism transitions preserve the smooth monodromy class.

The larger-root calculation is not a formal replacement of 1 by kl in the old vanishing statements. With z=kl, d=3k+4l, the horizontal line is O(d−12z), and O(d−13z) has H1 of dimension one exactly when k>1. Thus logarithmic tangent cohomology is (1,1,1,1) when k>1 and (1,1,0,0) when k=1. The dual residue of the monomial x^(3l−2)y^(k−2) is nonzero at the cusp. It makes the connecting map from H2(K_dP6) onto logarithmic H3 have rank one and gives absolute tangent cohomology (1,1,1,0) in every case. This is the concrete step missing from a naive extension of R4.

The signed canonical growth `s=1/12−1/(3k)−1/(4l)` determines the ordered pair: the reduced fraction `1/12−s=(m+n)/(mn)` recovers the sum and product because m,n are coprime. Cohomology jumping loci plus local miniversality prove separation in any single irreducible analytic family. That argument alone does not handle arbitrary chains. The additional geometric reconstruction and propagation in E.2.5–E.2.23 now supply the missing closedness and prove the full holomorphic deformation-chain assertion.

The later smooth-specialization argument constructs the unique relative line L with `L^(kl−3k−4l)=K_rel^(12kl)`. A finite free direct image supplies two independent central sections. The valid non-holomorphic-meromorphic-map theorem of Campana–Demailly–Peternell, independently written out by Lehn–Rollenske–Schinko, makes their ratio holomorphic on a complex six-sphere. For positive canonical slope, surface classification and connectedness of actual divisor limits remove the common fixed divisor and target degree loss. For negative slope, a hypothetical horizontal fixed divisor first forces irreducible fibres; fibrewise duality and horizontal twisting then give simultaneous generic Picard vanishings contradicting chi(TX)=1. Thus E.2.10 proves full pencil persistence for both signs. This uses no part of the invalid general holomorphic-fibration conclusion of CDP2020.

Once the relative fibration has extended, its moving critical-value sections have meromorphic projective normalization even when they collide centrally. A fixed regular target fibre is a genuine smooth torus family over the full disc, so has identity H1 monodromy. If `z=exp(−πic/(kl))` vanished to order d, its return matrix would instead be `S_(−2kld)` times a single peripheral power. Taking the third or fourth power eliminates either finite peripheral but not S; at the cusp the coefficient `u→u+n w` first forces n=0 and then forces d=0. Thus the zero end is excluded in this precise persistence setting. Boundedness and the maximum principle place the limiting period strictly inside the admissible disc. The separate chart and collar arguments in E.2.13–E.2.21 then identify the actual central manifold with that reference member.

For the two-parameter source, the given Weierstrass equation has a III* fibre, not III. The torus degeneration orders are (1,0), (1,0), (1,1), so only the first two nodal values yield rank-one fillings. The source itself says this correctly. The finite norm image is `4Z ell + 2Z delta`; a candidate map with fibre-linear scalar −1 fails its first congruence, and scalar +1 requires an even additive period shift. The explicit even-shift defects `(-h u/2, 0, h u/2, -h w/2)` obey the sphere relation. Their rank-one/rank-two collar regularity is imposed explicitly, rather than inferred solely from membership in a vanishing lattice. The difference bundle on P(1,4) is O⊕O(−1), whose H1 vanishes, completing the gluing.

## E.2 proof progression and superseded obstructions

1. Direct extension of old root acyclicity fails when k>1. This issue was resolved by the nonzero residue calculation and is not a remaining gap.
2. Local miniversality proves an open fixed-pair locus. It does not establish closedness in the full deformation space. A smooth limiting fibre outside the construction could have a larger, reducible Kuranishi germ.
3. Canonical and anticanonical section growth separates pairs in one irreducible family. It does not separate arbitrary chains: at a new intervening fibre the sections can jump on different branches, so equality of their generic values on one irreducible base cannot be carried through that fibre.
4. The earlier unrestricted zero-end concern is resolved for all one-sided smooth specializations in E.2.5: E.2.10 supplies the relative fibration for either slope, and E.2.7 excludes zero and places the limiting period in the interior.
5. The earlier possible indeterminate central pencil is superseded by E.2.5. The subsequent common-divisor and target-degree concerns are now resolved by E.2.6 and E.2.8–E.2.10 for both signs. For negative slope, a hypothetical horizontal fixed divisor forces rank-one fibre cohomology and irreducibility; simultaneous generic Picard vanishings then contradict chi(TX)=1. This does not assert a blanket exclusion of all holomorphic Hopf-surface fibrations. The exact unsuccessful normalization-classification routes remain in E_hopf_case_audit.md, marked as superseded for the fixed-divisor problem.
6. The additional-central-critical-value concern is resolved by E.2.11–E.2.12. Near a hypothetical such value, the explicit period construction extends the Jacobian; Hartogs extends its infinitesimal translations, compact averaging removes isotropy, and a finite transverse-map argument excludes an isolated critical image. A degenerate target normalization thus leaves an isotrivial fibration with one or two critical values. Its ordinary constant-image torus need not be abelian; its finite-root constant-image torus is an abelian surface.
7. The collapsed-target alternatives left by E.2.12 are now excluded. E.2.14 extends the normalized delta field through the smooth parameter family and preserves its fixed P1 with normal O(-1) plus O(-1). A finite-root constant image supplies a second independent period and hence an impossible nontrivial elliptic action with a fixed point. For an ordinary constant image, E.2.15 uses K^A=f*O(D), the constant torus fibre volume, and integrability of a positive A-canonical density to force D<-A, contradicting D/A>=-1/2.
8. The target remains noncollapsed, with three distinct critical values, and E.2.13 supplies the two smooth finite-root charts. E.2.16 then forces all three critical fibres irreducible and the cusp reduced. E.2.17 applies the strict numerical nonsplitting theorem of Lazzeri (1973), read in the original pp.274–275, to the two-dimensional local circle quotients. It proves relative normal crossings and simultaneous smooth proper normalization. The subsequent E.2.18–E.2.20 supply the separate analytic cusp identification; normal-crossing type alone is not treated as such an identification.
9. E.2.18 identifies the central normalized pair: contracting three alternating disjoint minus-one curves produces a triangle of self-intersection-one curves. Nef intersection excludes pluricanonical sections; the compact-surface classification identifies the contracted surface as P2. The relative toric logarithmic tangent frame follows from constant-cohomology base change. This avoids a simultaneous contraction theorem. Grauert's original analytic contraction statement was read in a full article transcription, with exact access distinctions recorded in E_normalization_source_audit.md.
10. E.2.19 extends the actual marked torus action. Descent matrices in a global normalized logarithmic frame are constant on compact conductor curves and equal the identity on the punctured parameter disc, hence also centrally. Grauert evaluation extends the commuting frame to the full cusp bidisc. The two toric periods extend by the implicit-function theorem, and their integral primitivity identifies them with the exact invariant lattice generated by w and delta. This supplies the action and its character data; E.2.20 performs the separate analytic toric-neighbourhood identification.
11. E.2.20 identifies the actual analytic cusp. The toric cover's simple connectedness is proved by its star-shaped charts and van Kampen. A primitive conductor stabilizer compares adjacent origins by an exact integral q-power times a holomorphic unit; finite paths exclude essential singularities. Unimodular cone coordinates then reconstruct the prescribed fan. Uniformity uses a finite compact fundamental component and deck-generator overlaps, rather than infinitely many independent neighbourhood radii. E.2.21 combines this with the finite affine charts and the additive collar cocycle.
12. E.2.21 completes the global one-sided identification: finite affine power classes and integral collar defects are constant in the parameter; after their normalization, the actual additive V cocycle is killed by the previously proved root averaging and holomorphic Laurent splitting. This yields the central member and a local holomorphic family comparison. The earlier concerns about uniqueness of a full punctured-disc smooth completion are resolved at this scope.
13. E.2.22 supplies the additional generic-disc propagation. The relative canonical root exists on the whole disc. Proper images of the actual base locus, critical-image defects, finite-root critical set, normalization defects and distinct-branch transversality failures form successive discrete analytic exceptional sets. The period-kernel identity is continued on the connected universal cover, avoiding an improper projection over the punctured target. Integral collar data are locally constant. The remaining isolated points are filled in reverse order by E.2.21; the argument never assumes the union of all successive exceptional sets is globally discrete.
14. E.2.23 passes to arbitrary analytic bases. A finite local projection with a unique fibre over the proposed closure point yields a complex curve through that point and a nearby known point. Its normalization is a connected Riemann surface, to which E.2.22 applies. This proves closedness without selecting a punctured disc inside an arbitrary open locus. Consequently distinct coprime pairs cannot be joined by arbitrary holomorphic deformation chains, while members of a fixed pair lie in the connected period family.

15. E.2.24 resolves the continuous-family interpretation as well. The original published Meersseman paper, Theorem 3.4 and Remark 3.6 on pp.898–899, gives the classical local Kuranishi retraction on a connected neighbourhood in the C-infinity space of integrable structures. E.2.23 applies to its connected analytic base even at an initially unknown structure. Thus a fixed-pair locus and its saturated quotient images are clopen. This is a separation theorem, not a claim that every pair has a unique marked connected component.

Question 1 is proved for arbitrary connected holomorphic deformation bases and chains, and for connected continuous families of integrable structures. Root and D independently reviewed E.2.22–E.2.24; the detailed referee notes are retained. Those reviews are verification of the same proofs, not additional mathematical methods. This section does not decide the remaining questions of Parts A–D, whose individual status rows remain controlling.

## E.4: completed independent certificate

All 19,701 boxes passed a new 192-bit Arb/acb implementation through python-flint 0.9.0. Only exact rational box endpoints were reused. No mpmath import, old radius, old preconditioner, old root, or old interval routine enters the new proof. The largest certified upper bound is enclosed by 1.999877571767410524070536478244687423578792301873142913 ± 9.26e-56, at zero-based input index 17866. Runtime was 49.0401 seconds. This is a rigorous enclosure, not a sample maximum.

An exact Fraction-based vertical sweep at 438 distinct vertical edges checks all 19,911 leaves: 19,701 norm boxes, 36 local boxes, 174 boxes outside the modular domain. Interiors are disjoint and the exact covered area is 23/40. The entire omitted square lies strictly in the independently treated analytic disc.

The analytic local proof was re-derived with all constants. Independent 256-bit Arb bounds give |G| < 18.372504033, |H| < 0.546229725, d in (10.03,10.04), g2(i)/4 in (47.26,47.30), Rouche lower bound >0.0321759437 and perturbation <0.0307366421, |tau-i|/|h|² <6.123343, |h|² <0.000044338219, and remainder coefficient <4399.024 <5000. The analytic cusp inequalities were also independently evaluated. Together these confirm R3's maximum and radius.

The program initially contained two dead, unreachable expressions while the half-integer theta sum was being entered; they were removed before the final rerun. They had no effect on the results. All operative calculations and final outputs are included. No input files are reproduced.

## Detailed record: root_literature_audit.md

## Root literature and source audit

The audit date is 15 November 2026. The attached original manuscript and attached arXiv versions are treated as mathematical inputs, not as verified authorities. Source-specific comparisons occur in the paper. No result is claimed to have priority on the basis of a search failing to find it.

### Smooth monodromy and characteristic numbers

Searches used the titles of Eells–Kuiper, Kervaire–Milnor and Cerf, and combinations of “mapping torus”, “homotopy sphere”, “spin-c Eells-Kuiper”, “equivariant diffeomorphism”, “semifree circle action” and “Blakers-Massey”. The primary scans of Eells–Kuiper (1962), Kervaire–Milnor (1963), Cerf (1970) and Poénaru (1971) were opened. The paper uses the exact statements and formulas recorded in its Part C and in C_push_report.md. The current arXiv source of Crowley–Nordström's Exotic G2-manifolds was checked for the spin-c normalization. Durán–Rigas, arXiv:0708.1577, §3.2, was read to test and reject the proposed inference from circle equivariance to a small mapping-class order.

The original Cerf1970 scan initially exceeded the web extractor's capacity; an independent local download succeeded, and primary page8 was inspected. The Γ4=0 book was bibliographically verified only and is explicitly marked not opened. It is not cited as the proof of the dimension-six identification. The scripts c_fetch_primary.py, c_review_primary.py and c_render_primary.py preserve the access record while leaving the scans and private OCR outside the deliverable.

Further searches and primary readings on circle actions, orbit knots and lift independence are documented in the C-related section notes and their bibliography. These establish comparison tools. They do not identify the annular twist of this family with an integer Haefliger invariant, and no numerical value of [Phi] is inferred from them.

### New trace obstruction and its limits

Queries included “Shioda On Mordell Weil lattices 1990 theorem 8.6 discriminant formula PDF”, “Oguiso Shioda Mordell Weil lattice rational elliptic surface 1991 pdf”, and the exact phrases “elliptic surface genus 3 I12 Mordell Weil”, “b^3 a^4 Mordell elliptic”, and “y^2=x^3 3b 2a elliptic surface”. The equation-specific searches did not locate the exact trace statement in B.3.4. This absence is not a claim of novelty.

The Rikkyo repository verified the original metadata of Shioda (1990), On the Mordell-Weil lattices, Commentarii Mathematici Universitatis Sancti Pauli39(2),211–240, DOI10.14992/00009994. Its direct PDF endpoint returned429. The primary author-uploaded PDF was then opened successfully at https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Tetsuji-Shioda/publication/247043151_On_the_Mordell-Weil_Lattices/links/5704b7fe08ae13eb88b69184/On-the-Mordell-Weil-Lattices.pdf . Theorems1.3,8.4,8.6 and8.7, Corollary5.3, and equations(8.11),(8.12),(8.20) were checked. The determinant relation follows from the narrow-lattice statement and its finite index.

Schütt and Shioda, Elliptic Surfaces, arXiv:0907.0298v3, was also opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/0907.0298 . Section11.10 equation(22) explicitly records the determinant relation; Section6.10 gives the Hodge numbers and Corollary6.13 the rank formula. This is corroboration by the originating author, not a substitute for checking the original height theorem. The concrete sections, intersection numbers, local infinity expansions, saturation congruences and trace obstruction in B.3.4 are calculated afresh in the accompanying exact programs.

The obstruction applies to a split elliptic normalization. A genus-one torsor without a section is a separate possibility. The report and statement preserve this distinction. Likewise, the polarized hypotheses in Mumford/Alexeev–Nakamura and the polarizable-variation hypothesis in analytic Néron-model results are not discarded when considering an arbitrary nonpolarized analytic filling.

### Interval arithmetic and boundary levels

The primary Arb paper, Johansson, arXiv:1611.02831, §§2–3, and the official python-flint Arb constructor documentation were opened. The E.4 proof gives its own series, geometric tail bounds and strict contraction argument; only rational rectangle endpoints were imported from the earlier certificate. The exact cover was checked separately with rational arithmetic. The D.4 certificate explicitly depends on these newly certified roots; it does not claim arithmetic independence from them. Its i-neighbourhood proof is analytic, and its rho/cusp code implements its own Fourier series and derivative bounds at256 bits. The compact part uses192-bit Arb enclosures and an exact refinement tree.

The D.4 source comparisons with Lin–Wang, Chen–Kuo–Lin and Chen–Fu–Lin are recorded in D_global_literature_audit.md and integrated into D.4.8. The distinction is between a Green function on a fixed torus and the composite modulus function evaluated at a zero of the Weierstrass function. The precise latter level-set theorem is proved by the included certificates and C1 level transport.

### Hodge obstruction and analytic cusp alternatives

Queries on 15 November 2026 included “six sphere semistable degeneration”, “normal crossing limit complex S6”, “Moishezon central fibre Hodge de Rham”, and “Conjecture 8.2 Hodge”. They did not locate the particular application proved in B.4.3. We make no priority claim. Chen's *On the Hodge Structures of Global Smoothings of Normal Crossing Varieties*, arXiv:2404.19229v2 (16 October 2026), was opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.19229 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19229v2 . Definition 2.3 and Theorem 2.4(4), including the K1 condition preceding parts (3)–(4), were read. Graf and Kovács, *Potentially Du Bois spaces* (2014), was opened at https://sites.math.washington.edu/~kovacs/current/papers/Graf_Kovacs__Potentially_Du_Bois_spaces.pdf ; §1.D, p.120, gives the proper Du Bois cohomology assertion. The exact hypotheses, derivation and remaining analytic scope are recorded in B_hodge_report.md and the independent E_B_hodge_referee.md.

To test whether the unproved class-C hypothesis might follow automatically, we searched “non Kahler semistable degeneration K3 Hopf Nishiguchi” and “Toric Kato manifolds degeneration”. We opened Nishiguchi's original MPI/88-48 preprint, *Degeneration of K3 surfaces, II*, at https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/3390/1/preprint_1988_48.pdf . Theorem 1.4 and Example 2.1 explicitly describe the non-Kähler alternative. We also opened Istrati, Otiman, Pontecorvo, and Ruggiero's *Toric Kato manifolds*, Journal de l'École polytechnique—Mathématiques 9 (2022), 1347–1395, DOI10.5802/jep.208, at https://www.numdam.org/item/JEP_2022__9__1347_0.pdf . Theorem 7.1 and its construction were read. These sources are cautionary analogues, not an S6 construction. A further search for “semistable degeneration compact complex manifolds first Betti number irregularity central components” did not provide the needed converse forcing arbitrary vertical components into class C.

### Annular twist, graphing and framing

Roseman and Takase, *High-codimensional knots spun about manifolds*, arXiv:math/0609055v2, was opened and §2.2, Theorem 2.1, §4 and Remark 5.1 were checked. Its graphing construction and characteristic formula concern a specified framed embedding. Koytcheff's *Graphing, homotopy groups of spheres, and spaces of long links and knots* (2025) was opened in the original Cambridge publication; Remark 5.9 supplies an associated-graded finite-type analogy with the Casson invariant, not an identification of the present annular orbit map with a classical torus knot. The primary-page comparisons are also recorded in the agents' C reports. The numbers obtained from a classical T(3,4) knot or an unrelated Brieskorn sphere were not assigned to the present monodromy. No comparison proving that assignment was found.

Engel's original Remark 8.3, printed p.23, was reopened and compared directly with the actual cusp periods. The literal extension of the quotient by translational ker γ leaves is false in the scope stated there: C.3.12 proves that every Hausdorff-valued continuous extension constant on those regular leaves collapses the entire cusp fibre. The supplied notes already recorded the simpler raw-character nonextension; that provenance is acknowledged. Earlier attempt notes which inferred an actual marked S³ quotient from the remark are retained but expressly superseded. C.3.13 then constructs a different, corrected map by an integral one-form extension which changes those leaves near the cusp. Its three-dimensional target framing is proved; the six-dimensional comparison remains open.

For B.4.5 we additionally searched “normal crossing H1 Kähler Friedman 1.5”, “proper normal crossing Dolbeault normalization surjective”, and “normal crossings ddbar cohomology analytic”. Friedman (1983), *Global smoothings of varieties with normal crossings*, was located at the original Annals metadata page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/1983/118-1/p06 ; the text of Proposition 1.5 was not opened and is not used as a proved premise. The direct analytic degree-one proof in B.4.5 instead constructs the branch resolutions, justifies local analytic exactness and lifts a Dolbeault cocycle. This removes the former simple-crossing restriction without importing an unchecked theorem.

### Citation format and evidence retention

The bibliography uses author–date citations and records opened/not-opened status in every entry. The official CTAN apacite page was checked as an available APA6 format implementation; it is a typesetting source, not a mathematical reference. Web result files in evidence contain metadata and retrieval identifiers only. Full copyrighted page bodies, primary scans and private OCR extracts are not republished.

## Detailed record: root_B_analytic_attempts.md

## Further attack on the unrestricted analytic filling

This record concerns the actual family after arbitrary finite parameter base change. It does not weaken the open problem to a polarized or toroidal subclass. The status remains Open.

The marked map to the t-line extends meromorphically by B.4.4. One proposed next step was to compare a graph modification with the explicitly constructed local toroidal models and conclude that every central normalization is class C. That comparison is not proved. A punctured isomorphism between proper families need not extend meromorphically: multiplication by exp(1/s) on the projective-line product gives an explicit elementary obstruction. The example invalidates the general extension principle, not the existence of the S6 filling. A special argument controlling the actual comparison is still required.

Kajiwara, Kato, and Nakayama (2008), original journal PDF, Theorem 0.1 and Section5.1, especially Theorem5.1.3, were opened at https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms150104.pdf . The proper-model construction does allow unpolarized log complex tori. It therefore improves on the hypothesis of a polarized Mumford theorem. It nevertheless presupposes a log torus with an admissible pairing. The missing global log extension and marked affine-torsor comparison are not supplied by that theorem. No claim that it classifies arbitrary proper analytic fillings is made.

Kamenova's original four-page author manuscript was opened at https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~kamenova/homepage_files/degen.pdf . Its Theorem3.1 assumes a Kähler total space and trivial canonical bundle. These conditions have not been established for a surface slice of an arbitrary filling here. The undated manuscript is cited as n.d.; an unverified publication date is not invented.

Schröer and Siebert (2002), original manuscript at https://www.math.uni-duesseldorf.de/~schroeer/publications_pdf/kodaira.pdf , introduction and Section3 Proposition3.1, were checked. The author bibliography supplies the published collection and pages193–222. Hopf normalizations in their construction belong to degenerations of primary Kodaira surfaces, so that construction is not a degeneration of the present regular torus fibres. It is retained as an analytic alternative to examine, without changing the nearby family.

The already-read Nishiguchi example was reopened at Example2.1. It shows why proper semistability alone does not imply class-C normalization, even for another family with Kähler nearby fibres. It does not demonstrate that a Hopf component occurs in this S6 problem. The finite-orbit fan components in A.2.4 are all class C: mixed components are complete toric threefolds, and pure-top components are bimeromorphic to the stated ruled bundle over S. The analytic unramified torsor alternative is still Kähler by B.3.8.

A further candidate is a non-Kähler cusp component with rational attaching surfaces. A naive fundamental-group argument only excludes attachments that leave a component loop alive. It must not be used when a Kato divisor in the overlap can kill that loop. The stronger degree-one comparison criterion now being checked in B.4.6 explicitly follows the maps on the overlaps and addresses that restricted construction. It does not force an unknown analytic component into a toric-Kato class.

The attempted horizontal classification also remains unproved. Non-isotrivial elliptic torsors over the genus-three cover are Kähler, but it has not been shown that every horizontal normalization of an arbitrary filling is such a ruled torsor model. Meromorphic extension of the base map alone does not give this identification. An analytic MMP or a generalized Albanese construction cannot be invoked with unstated Kähler or Hodge-degeneration hypotheses.

Searches on 15 November2026 included: “proper analytic degeneration semistable central fibre non Kahler bimeromorphic extensions uniqueness Moishezon graph”; “degeneration complex tori Hopf surfaces semistable Ueno Nakamura”; “analytic semistable degenerations complex tori non Kahler central components classification”; “analytic Néron complex tori degeneration”; and the exact KKN, Kamenova and Schröer–Siebert titles. These searches did not supply the required unrestricted comparison. This is a search record, not a priority or nonexistence claim.

## Root check of the all-fan topology proof

The source of A.4.8 was read in full. The original AKMW Theorems1.6.1 and2.7.1 and their finite polyhedral proof on p552, analytic correspondence in Section5.5 p565, and barycentric construction in Section5.6 were opened at https://personal.math.ubc.ca/~karu/papers/semist.pdf . Matsuki's original erratum, Section2 p2, was opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0006038 . The invalid strong-factorization algorithm is not used. The proof factors finite strict cone complexes, then realizes their monomial blow-ups analytically. Temporary pure-q blow-ups are explicitly allowed and their noncompact centres have no top cohomology contribution. This is the scope required for the claimed rank invariants. The result is accepted in the stated toroidal category, independently of the separately preserved D referee.

## Root C framing diagnostics

The proposed shortcut that the quotient torus gamma-u-w bounds a four-chain inside the cusp body was rejected after E's check: it represents the central oriented normalization three-cycle and need not bound. The relative-Chern-class approach therefore still requires a global chain and an actual comparison of stable frames. No Pontryagin number or cap integer is inferred from the local affine frame.

The exact program root_C_exterior_h3.py computes A0 and the two third-exterior-power norm matrices. Their stacked rank is3, and their common kernel is generated rationally by (0,1,1,6) in the basis (gamma-u-w, gamma-u-delta, gamma-w-delta, u-w-delta). The gamma-u-w class has nonzero norm at both finite places. These are exact lattice diagnostics; integral homology of the finite quotients is not silently identified with invariant lattices.

For a possible global-chain construction, the cusp-complement orbit space is examined as a pushout of its two oriented three-dimensional finite orbit fibres along the regular three-torus. The degree-three cover and degree-four cover give the top-dimensional relation (3,-4), so the regular three-torus has coefficient12 in its resulting rank-one cokernel. There is an additional degree-three term from the degree-two kernel, so this observation is not a complete homology or framing calculation. The coefficient12 agrees with C.3.16, but has not been assigned to the missing characteristic integer J.

## Fifth-hour continuation and superseded C stopping point

The preceding C diagnostics are historical. C.3.18–C.3.22 subsequently constructed the actual chain, identified its circle line, and calibrated both signs. C.3.24 now proves the actual one-loop class zero, and C.3.25–C.3.27 give a second invariant conversion and direct integer-additivity proof. The old unproved shortcuts remain rejected; they were not promoted into the completed proof.

For B, the automatic-comparison approach was checked against Biliotti–Ghigi, Meersseman, and the definability discussion of Katzarkov–Lee–Lupercio–Meersseman. The exact hypotheses fail or have not been proved here, as recorded in B_comparison_literature.tex and evidence/root_B_comparison_sources.md. The abstract C* automorphism group does not make its action meromorphic: its nonconstant orbit inside a regular compact torus cannot extend to P1. This is a direct obstruction to that shortcut.

B.4.7 is a complete proper semistable Hopf family, with a simply connected centre and h1(O)=1. Its total neighbourhood and the constant product have different fundamental groups, so there is no proper meromorphic comparison over both. It isolates an actual mechanism left outside the ddbar obstruction. Its nearby fibre is not S6. The candidate exceptional elliptic interface can acquire a triple divisor, so the normal-degree −1/0 mismatch by itself does not exclude all attachments.

Fujimoto's logarithmic transformations give the established cocycle machinery behind that example. The original 1988 Example3.4(1) also supplies an elliptic abelian product comparison. Nakayama2002 Theorem7.1.3 answers the associated elliptic codimension-two extension question under its precise hypotheses. Neither applies directly to a simple two-torus family. A broader proposed Cartier surgery with values in the full varying two-torus must use a monodromy-compatible integral cycle. Its compatibility across the two degenerating axes is being checked; it is not ruled out merely by generic simplicity. Possible non-toric blow-ups along elliptic curves and non-Kähler caps with higher intersections remain construction candidates, not constructed fillings or assigned topologies.
