## Detailed record: root_ninth_hour_audit.md

# Ninth-hour root audit

Date: 15 November 2026. These are further AI mathematical reviews and source checks, not human verification or a claim of priority. The global analytic filling in Part B remains open.

## The resolved local corner, B.4.22

Root and boundary_d read the complete proof of the local resolution of fuv=g s^n, with r=g-f. For n=1 the two stated blowups have the three smooth charts written in the proof. Their r-central divisors are respectively f(Uv-1)=0, f(G-1)=0 and g(1-F)=0, hence are reduced normal crossings. The pullback q=s^n creates an A_(n-1) product chart and the toric chart uvF=s^n. The unit triangulation of n times the standard triangle has n^2 unimodular cones, and its F-nonzero boundary is the stated minimal A-resolution. The subdivision therefore glues to the adjacent chart. Near F=1, F=(1+H)^(-1) identifies the construction with the actual repaired Tate neighbourhood from B.4.21 and r=fH.

This is a local fourfold resolution with an exact collar comparison. It does not supply the whole second period or a proper cap. The s-central divisor is reduced, while the q-central divisor has multiplicity n; these are not interchanged. The initial failed polynomial equality check compared expanded and unexpanded expressions and was corrected explicitly. Its original output, the correction and the passing exact calculation are retained.

## Exceptional curves obstruct the fixed-base repair, B.4.23

Root and limits_ab read the complete source. The marked exceptional curve meets the persistent elliptic axis at the point represented by -s^12. Its positive loop has nonzero class (12,0), and a finite cover of degree d changes this to (12d,0). An analytic divisor closure would intersect the elliptic-axis family in a proper degree-one horizontal curve. Normalization would produce an extending section, contradicting that winding. Pushing a closure down through a finite sequence of proper modifications gives the same contradiction. Thus the obstruction is not merely the failure of one coordinate formula.

The separate deformation argument assumes that the exceptional curve is disjoint from every conductor and that the original q-map is a submersion there. Its normal sequence is an extension of O^2 by O(-1). The vanishing of H^1(O(-1)) splits it, so the Douady space is smooth of dimension two and maps locally isomorphically to the two original base parameters. The curve would persist in ordinary nearby two-tori, which contain no rational curve. The conductor and fixed-base hypotheses are necessary and remain in the statement.

Root opened and read the original author version of Dloussky and Teleman, Infinite bubbling in non-Kählerian geometry, Proposition 2.3 and its proof on printed pages 6–7. Its local Douady argument requires a holomorphic submersion of relative dimension two, not global properness of the family, and directly supports the deformation step used here. The published metadata are Mathematische Annalen 353(4), 1283–1314 (2012), DOI 10.1007/s00208-011-0713-9. Kodaira's 1962 metadata alone were not treated as an opened proof. The auxiliary Merkulov publisher extract was read by boundary_d, while root's attempted DOI open failed; the fully read Dloussky–Teleman proposition is the direct primary support.

## The actual logarithmic torsor, B.4.24

Root and boundary_d read the complete proof. The multiplicative period pairing has columns (q a0,a1^(-1)) and (a1^6,q r^12 a2). Its sharp pairing is diagonal with entries q and q+12r. Every face passes admissibility; residual pairings are nondegenerate after shrinking. Root read Kajiwara, Kato and Nakayama's original Logarithmic abelian varieties, Sections 1.2–1.3, Theorem 5.2.1 and Theorem 5.3.2. These permit the associated log complex torus and its proper fan models without assuming a polarization.

The choice of extending axial origins is decisive. On the reduced q-axis model the actual section sigma is holomorphic in q and gives the origin. On the rooted rotating model the identity gives the other origin. Thus the difference of the two associated group origins is F, although the raw comparison of analytic covering coordinates is zeta-sigma+F. This does not revive the previously withdrawn claim that F alone obstructs every raw analytic comparison. Its r-loop has Y-component gamma. On the q-axis neighbourhood, the ghost map sends u and gamma to (12,0) and (0,12), so any extending log-torus section has zero r-loop Y-component. On the other neighbourhood the r-loop contracts. No sum of two extending sections can therefore split F. After finite coordinate roots the obstruction is n gamma, still nonzero.

This excludes locally trivial torsor extensions under the specified associated log-torus sheaf. A fan model is not assumed to be a group object. No implication from an arbitrary analytic cap to such a torsor has been proved.

## The one-eigenvalue Hopf boundary, B.4.25

Root and limits_ab independently read the full proof. For the diagonal Hopf family with multipliers alpha=q^m kappa(q) and beta(q), where 0<|beta(0)|<1, bivector automorphy gives alpha^(i-1) beta^(j-1)=1. Under |alpha|<|beta|<1 this leaves zw and, precisely when alpha=beta^N, the additional monomial w^(N+1). Thus the anticanonical dimension is exactly one or two. Quantitative Rouché estimates with radius (2|beta(0)|^N/|kappa(0)|)^(1/m) produce distinct resonances tending to zero for arbitrarily large N.

For a hypothetical smooth proper completion, M=pi_* K_total^(-1) is torsion-free and hence locally free on the disc. The torsion of the coherent sheaf R^1 pi_* K_total^(-1) has locally finite support. The direct-image exact sequence for multiplication by q-a therefore makes the anticanonical dimension constant on a sufficiently small punctured disc. This contradicts the accumulating resonances. Reduction, retention of the dominating component and a resolution which is an isomorphism over the smooth punctured family extend the contradiction to arbitrary proper analytic completions. The proof also covers every finite base change.

Root and boundary_d read Oda's original Torus embeddings and applications, Section 13, printed pages 113–118. In its basis v1=n, v2=-n+a n', the period n'=(v1+v2)/a becomes (t,t) after lambda=t^a. Both contraction eigenvalues tend to zero. That construction does not contradict B.4.25, whose second eigenvalue has nonzero limit. Oda's Theorem 14.1, printed pages 121–122, was also read for the degree-one parabolic Inoue surface. It is not used to claim a completion of the actual torus family.

## Every origin component must be vertical, B.4.26

Root and boundary_d independently read the full proof. A finite multisection of positive degree d would have a trace in each of the two actual axial group origins. At a point of either punctured axis, the normalized finite cover is a disjoint union of cyclic root covers x=t^e, after shrinking away the non-axis branch curves. The ordinary trace extends across the ordinary branch locus. In a toric chart each character is t^k times a holomorphic unit. Its norm is (-1)^(k(e-1)) x^k times a holomorphic unit. This proves extension as a bounded log character, with integral orders and the correct period ambiguity; the first character has zero r-axis order. The two traces would differ by dF, whose r-loop has nonzero Y-component d gamma. B.4.24 forbids this.

A point of local fibre dimension two in a fourfold over the bidisc gives a finite dominating two-dimensional slice by two generic local linear sections. A finite representative over a smaller complete bidisc and its normalization would supply exactly the forbidden multisection. Thus no such point exists. Every component of the origin fibre has dimension at least two, while flatness over r bounds its dimension by three. All origin components must consequently be three-dimensional.

The map to the fixed bidisc is required to be holomorphic. An initially meromorphic q-map must first be resolved by a graph modification, retaining its resulting geometry. The theorem does not exclude vertical components, which are precisely the remaining possibility.

## Point blowups cannot repair the logarithmic torsor, B.4.27

Root and boundary_d read the full source. On a sequence of point blowups S over the origin, the exact bounded-character sheaf is M_(D_Q+E)^gp times M_(D_Q+D_R+E)^gp. The missing strict-r divisor in the first factor is essential. The two-chart calculation gives vanishing of higher O-cohomology. The exceptional classes form an integral Picard basis, since their intersection matrix is unimodular. The divisor-order sequence then gives H^1=0 for each factor and H^2 equal to one integer per exceptional curve.

For q=Q^(12m), r=R^n, and exceptional valuations (a_i,b_i), the map from H^2(Y) to H^2 of the bounded-character sheaf has blocks diag(12m a_i,12m a_i+12n b_i). Both entries are positive, so the map is injective over the integers. The quotient sequence proves H^1(S,A_S)=0. An extension of the actual nontrivial punctured torsor would therefore become trivial on restriction, a contradiction. The N=0 restriction is written as beta^(-1)(B minus {0}), avoiding identification of B minus an empty exceptional union with the punctured bidisc. Root opened and read the exact pullback statement, KKN Lemma 1.2.9, printed pages 80–81. The 124 finite marked-blowup checks supplement, and do not replace, the general proof.

## What the original logarithmic Picard theorem assumes

Root read Kajiwara, Kato and Nakayama's original Analytic log Picard varieties, Sections 7.1–7.3, the hypotheses preceding Section 8, Assumption 8.1, Theorem 8.2, and Sections 9.1–9.4 and 10.2–10.5. Goodness for H^1 includes the comparison, filtration and log Hodge conditions in Section 7.1. Section 8 additionally assumes exactness, torsion-free characteristic cokernels and the degree-one local invariant-cycle assertion. Section 9.3 establishes goodness under the stated projective or log-Kähler hypotheses. Proposition 10.5 supplies a log Albanese mapping result with projectivity, verticality and the other listed hypotheses. None is silently applied to an arbitrary cap here. In particular the full log Albanese universal property is posed as a problem before that partial result.

## Remaining approaches and exact failures

The explicit corner resolution still lacks a global second-period action. In the linearized Tate coordinate Z=xG(H), the desired second period requires G(H)/G(bH), with b=q r^12 times a unit. Although bH is regular at the new F=0 charts, the numerator has an infinite zero pattern under continuation beyond the collar. No proper global quotient has been constructed from that expression.

Adding the persistent elliptic axis as a further conductor would remove the O-valued r-deformation direction of the exceptional rational curve, so B.4.23 would no longer apply there. Pairing the degree-one axis with an anticanonical elliptic curve in a degree-one del Pezzo surface gives a candidate with smoothing Euler number 12 over a fixed ordinary q, whereas an ordinary torus has Euler number zero. It therefore does not supply the required fixed-base nearby surface. A changed map involving exceptional base directions remains a different, unconstructed possibility.

B.4.26 now requires every missing-origin component to be vertical. A construction that uses a Kato parameter s must therefore allow the original q to vanish identically on such a component, for example through a local relation q=r^a h(s), rather than identifying s with the original q on the central divisor. Compactification over the resulting exceptional direction and the exact punctured-family comparison have not been obtained.

Searches for a maximal-monodromy classification of arbitrary analytic degenerations of two-tori again found the Kähler/canonical hypotheses in Kamenova's Theorem 3.1 and the K3-specific scope in Nishiguchi's constructions. They do not establish the required unrestricted comparison. An anticanonical-pair charge argument is being tested, but obtaining a log-canonical model from an arbitrary analytic filling is itself unproved. These failures remain part of the work record, rather than being promoted to a nonexistence theorem.

The ninth-hour source metadata record includes the exact Oda, KKN, Dloussky–Teleman, Ueno/Fujimoto and anticanonical-pair search routes. External scans and extracted full article text remain outside the deliverable archive.

Root additionally opened Doi's original 2009 journal PDF, Theorem 1.1, Example 5.3, Section 5.3 and Theorem 5.5, printed pages 854 and 880–883. The latter constructs a smooth real total six-manifold and a smooth map, with continuously varying complex surface fibres. It does not assert a holomorphic total space or the prescribed family comparison. The degree-zero elliptic ruled example yields a torus, while nonzero degree yields a Kodaira surface. This scope check is integrated into the comparison remark in the paper.

## Build correction

Draft31 compiled all four LuaLaTeX/BibTeX stages with zero overfull boxes and integrated 148 statements. The consistency audit correctly rejected its remaining undefined citation Grauert1960D: the assembled bibliography uses the established alias Grauert60push for that same primary article. The B.4.25 citation was changed to the retained key. The failed audit and all compiler logs are preserved; this is a bibliography-key correction, not a mathematical change.

## A conditional torus classification and its substantive correction, B.4.28

Root read the entire first candidate and then the entire revised argument. Boundary_d's independent review correctly rejected the candidate's free-product formula for the central fundamental group. Although each normalized rational double curve is simply connected, a triple-point attaching relation can include a path around the whole conductor cycle inside a component. For an Inoue–Hirzebruch component that path can represent its nontrivial fundamental-group generator. The component group need not survive as a free factor. The attempted strengthening using the same formula for arbitrary rational conductors was also withdrawn. The exact rejected passage is retained with a REJECTED warning, and no Proved heading, in evidence/ab_push_log_canonical_rejected_free_product.tex. The earlier B.4.6 does not use that formula: its comparison proof explicitly includes the d2 map in the five-term spectral sequence, and remains unaffected.

The repaired B.4.28 assumes, in addition to a proper semistable degeneration of two-tori with rank(T-1)=2, that the central dualizing line is trivial. Adjunction gives reduced anticanonical conductor pairs. Their open Euler contributions are nonnegative, and the nearby torus has Euler number zero. This leaves toric rational pairs, elliptic ruled or Hopf pairs, and corner blow-ups of hyperbolic or parabolic Inoue pairs. The sum of elliptic normal degrees is zero by d-semistability, whereas every parabolic component contributes a strictly negative degree and all other surviving elliptic types contribute zero; therefore parabolic components are excluded. If every conductor is elliptic, annular twists show that the rank of T-1 is at most the cycle rank of a segment or circle, hence at most one. This excludes the elliptic case.

For a remaining hyperbolic Inoue component, Oda's explicit toric cover supplies a compact T2 mapping torus in its complement of the conductor, with hyperbolic matrix A. Its angular fibre can be chosen in a node cone, where it is exactly the local Clifford torus. Corner blow-ups change the unimodular coordinate basis, not this lattice or A. A compact-core trivialization embeds that marked mapping torus into the nearby surface. Its two fibre-circle images vanish in rational H1 because A-1 is invertible. Since the cohomology ring of a complex two-torus is the exterior algebra on H1, its angular T2 has zero rational H2 image.

The trivial central dualizing line has one global section, as does the canonical line on every nearby torus. Proper analytic base change gives a relative dualizing section whose evaluation is nonzero on the whole central fibre. In xyz=t coordinates its relative two-form is U(x,y,z) dx/x wedge dy/y with U(0) nonzero. Integration on the very same angular torus gives (2pi i)^2 sum a_(k,k,k)t^k, tending to the nonzero iterated residue. This contradicts the zero H2 image. It excludes the hyperbolic components using the nearby torus, without the rejected central fundamental-group claim. The remaining toric pairs give a closed oriented dual surface; the residue signs orient it, and coherent Euler characteristic zero gives genus one.

Root and boundary_d passed this revised full proof. Root opened the exact originals used: Friedman, On the geometry of anticanonical pairs, Lemma 2.7 and its preceding charge definitions; Dloussky, On surfaces of class VII with numerically anticanonical divisor, Proposition 2.27 and proof, printed pages 17–19 in the author version; Fujiki and Pontecorvo, Sections 3.2–3.3, for the reduced anticanonical alternatives; and Oda, Theorem 15.1 and the following complement description, printed pages 130–131. The result does not produce a trivial-dualizing model from an arbitrary analytic degeneration.

## Invariant forms do not determine an affine comparison, B.4.29

Root and boundary_d read the entire explicit counterexample. The fibre product of two Tate families has one conifold xy=uv. Its two small-resolution charts have t=xAv and t=uBy, giving a smooth proper semistable total threefold. Both elliptic factors degenerate, so H1 monodromy has rank two. Translation of its first factor by exp(2pi i/t) leaves all relative invariant forms and the Gauss–Manin data unchanged. It also preserves the extending absolute canonical form dt wedge dZ/Z wedge dW/W. Nevertheless the images of the zero section along t=1/j and t=1/(j+1/2) tend to distinct smooth central points, with first multiplicative coordinates 1 and -1.

A hypothetical proper meromorphic graph, pulled back along the extending zero section, would have a unique dominating proper analytic curve. Its normalization is finite of degree one over the disc, hence is the disc itself. It would extend the translated section holomorphically, contradicting the two limits. Thus the failure is genuinely meromorphic, not merely holomorphic. The choice h(t)=exp(1/t) gives an essential horizontal term in a chosen absolute one-form lift while preserving the same relative and canonical data. Missing spacing-command backslashes in the first TeX draft were corrected before integration.

This is a counterexample to control of a marked affine origin by linear invariant-form data. It does not refute extension of the difference of two sections which both extend into the same cap; a common essential translation cancels from that difference. The latter question remains an active possible route to the main unrestricted filling problem.


## Inspected checkpoint build and hourly decision

Before this checkpoint, root independently opened Illusie, Kato and Nakayama (2005), Theorems 6.2 and 6.3, printed pages 32 and 34, and read the full hypotheses and conclusion. The ordinary logarithmic de Rham comparison and locally free nilpotent extension do not assume a Kähler or projective total space. The 2007 erratum concerns Theorem 7.1, and no general analytic Hodge-degeneration theorem is being inferred. A working proof using saturation, integral local meridians, relative primitives, multiplied traces and Hartogs extension is retained in B_two_section_bridge_working.md. It is not a numbered theorem in this checkpoint.

The exact reduction from an arbitrary filling was separately re-audited in ab_push_marked_graph_scope.md. The intrinsic marked meromorphic q-map extends by resolving its graph, preserving the given r-nonzero family. The resulting map is flat away from finitely many central q-values, so its flattening near the cusp may be supported at the missing origin. It need not preserve the prescribed model on r=0, q nonzero. The full-rank two-section argument currently applies at exceptional divisors, not to that rank-one axial comparison. Consequently the proposed origin argument concerns completion of the fixed partial family B.4.19, and cannot yet be enlarged to nonexistence of arbitrary r-only fillings.

The ninth-hour checkpoint build is draft33: 220 pages, 150 statement rows and 113 bibliography entries. All four compiler stages succeeded with zero overfull boxes; the integration audit passes without unresolved references, citations or rerun warnings. 29 distinct rendered pages were visually inspected, including all ninth-hour proof pages. The exact record is qa/draft33/visual_inspection.json. Draft31 had no overfull box but failed its citation audit on an obsolete Grauert key; the alias was corrected and its failed audit is retained. The ninth-hour assessment is CONTINUE. Checkpoint09 is scheduled for 21:38:56 UTC and is not created earlier.

## Detailed record: D_B4428_referee.md

# Independent referee of the B.4.28 candidate

Referee: boundary_d. Full initial source read: `sections/ab_push_log_canonical_torus.tex`, 2026-11-15, approximately 21:12 UTC.

## Initial verdict: a substantive gap in the proposed fundamental-group step

The component classification, nonnegative open Euler charges, elimination of interior blowups, elliptic normal-degree balance, and annular-twist rank bound are consistent with the stated extra hypothesis that the central dualizing bundle is trivial. However, the initial formula expressing pi1(D) as a free product of one Z per hyperbolic Inoue component and pi1 of the dual complex is not justified and must not be used.

Although each individual rational double curve is simply connected, paths through a whole conductor cycle can represent a nontrivial element of the normalization component's fundamental group. Triple-point attaching relations retain these path elements. In a hyperbolic Inoue component, a conductor cycle lifts to an infinite chain under the cyclic toric cover; its cycle loop therefore maps nontrivially to the component's infinite cyclic group. Edge groups being trivial does not eliminate this twisting in the complex-of-spaces presentation.

The diagnostic is concrete: replacing each split hyperbolic vertex's two deleted discs by an annulus can give a connected closed surface of Euler characteristic zero, and hence an abelian Z^2 fundamental group. Thus the proposed free-product contradiction is not a harmless omission. This was communicated to limits_ab and root immediately, and limits_ab independently confirmed and withdrew the step.

## Root's replacement: a local residue and hyperbolic-link contradiction

The following replacement has been independently checked and does not require a global torus-bundle assembly.

1. A sufficiently small closed boundary of either rational cycle in a hyperbolic Inoue component is a T2 mapping torus with hyperbolic monodromy A in SL2(Z). It lies entirely in the complement of the conductor. A compact neighbourhood of this boundary transports into each nearby smooth fibre by the submersion theorem.
2. Its T2 fibre can be chosen as the local angular torus at a node of the conductor cycle. In Oda's toric cover, the compact angular torus is N_R/N. The adjacent rays at a smooth toric node form an integral basis; the dual characters x,y identify its angular torus precisely with |x|=epsilon1, |y|=epsilon2. A fundamental path in the moment space, parallel to the infinite boundary chain and joining a point to its A-image, can be chosen through that cone point. Its compact-torus inverse image is the required mapping torus.
3. Corner blowups are periodic toric star subdivisions. They do not change the dense torus, the deck matrix A, or the angular T2; they only replace its coordinate basis by a unimodular basis. The same marking therefore holds for every allowed corner blowup.
4. The mapping-torus relations imply `(A-I)v=0` for the images of the two fibre circles in the abelian fundamental group of a nearby complex two-torus. Since A is hyperbolic, A-I is invertible over Q, so both images vanish rationally. The induced H2 class of the angular torus is zero: H2 of a real four-torus is dual to the products of H1 classes, and all such products pull back to zero.
5. The proper family is Gorenstein and h0 of its relative dualizing restriction is one on the central fibre and on all nearby two-tori. Grauert base change extends a nonzero generator of the trivial central dualizing bundle to a relative section, which remains nonvanishing near the compact centre.
6. At the corresponding triple point the local equation is xyz=t. The relative section is `U(x,y,z) dlog(x) wedge dlog(y)`, where U(0,0,0) is nonzero. Its integral over `|x|=epsilon1, |y|=epsilon2, z=t/(xy)` is `(2 pi i)^2` times the diagonal coefficient series `sum a_nnn t^n`, hence is nonzero for sufficiently small t. This contradicts the zero rational homology class from the mapping-torus relation.

This excludes the hyperbolic components directly in the nearby torus and repairs the conditional classification once written into the source. The former central free-product formula is not repaired or retained.

## Primary source opened for the exact angular marking

Oda, T. (1978). *Lectures on Torus Embeddings and Applications*. TIFR Lectures 58. Original full PDF: https://mathweb.tifr.res.in/Documents/Publications/Lectures/tifr58.pdf . Section15, Theorem15.1 and its proof, printed pp.130–131, describe the toric cover, its two infinite boundary chains, the hyperbolic unit deck action, and explicitly identify the complement of the two cycles as R times a compact angular-torus bundle over S1. Current retrieval records: turn631view0, turn632view0, turn632view1. The lattice/character identification in items 2–3 is the direct toric-coordinate calculation, not a claim that the source states a general semistable-plumbing theorem.

Fujiki, A., & Pontecorvo, M. (2010). *Anti-self-dual bihermitian structures on Inoue surfaces*. Journal of Differential Geometry, 85, 15–71. Original full article opened at https://archive.ymsc.tsinghua.edu.cn/pacm_download/21/834-JDG-85-1-A2-fujiki.pdf ; Sections3.2–3.3 confirm the topology, anticanonical configurations and corner-blowup conventions. Current retrieval records: turn630view0–2, turn631view1–2.

The extra central-dualizing triviality hypothesis remains essential to this argument. No K-trivial reduction for arbitrary analytic completions has been supplied.

## Final revised-source verdict

The revised source was reread in full at the changed hyperbolic and final dual-complex sections. **PASS.** It now contains the marked hyperbolic mapping torus, the compact-core transport, the H1-to-H2 vanishing in the actual nearby two-torus, and the exact convergent diagonal-series residue integral. The false free-product formula is absent from the proposition and retained only as a rejected historical attempt. The final rational toric dual complex is a closed oriented surface with coherent Euler characteristic zero, hence a torus. The extra central-dualizing triviality assumption remains explicit.

Bibliographic precision suggested to the author: use the already opened constant-dimension base-change theorem BS76push, III Theorem4.12, or the established Grauert60push alias. A suppressed Grauert1960D key and a generic Section7 reference should not be propagated into the assembled bibliography.

## Detailed record: D_B4429_referee.md

# Independent referee of B.4.29

Referee: boundary_d. Full source read: `sections/B_forms_translation_counterexample.tex`, 2026-11-15, approximately 21:18 UTC.

**Verdict: PASS.**

The fibre product of two proper nodal Tate families has the single local conifold xy=uv. The indicated small resolution is proper and isomorphic away from that point, so it glues to the unchanged complement. Its two smooth charts have parameter xAv and uBy; each central equation is reduced normal crossing. Nearby fibres remain products of two elliptic curves, and the two independent rank-one nilpotent blocks give rank two on H1 and H^1.

The essential translation is a holomorphic automorphism over the punctured disc. Its images of the zero section have the stated distinct central smooth limits at the two explicit sequences. Restricting a hypothetical proper meromorphic graph to that section yields a proper analytic space over the disc. Its unique dominating component is a curve and its normalization is finite of degree one over the normal disc. Hence the translated section would extend holomorphically, contradicting the limits. Vertical graph components do not evade this argument.

All relative invariant forms and integral cohomology are fixed, so the induced map on the entire ordinary relative-form bundle and Gauss–Manin bundle is the identity. The coherent lattices arising from the same proper family are therefore literally the same under either marking. The explicit multiples of relative forms are regular in the two resolution charts.

The absolute canonical form calculation also checks in both charts: dt wedge dZ/Z wedge dW/W becomes dv wedge dx wedge dA and dy wedge dB wedge du, respectively. The added horizontal translation derivative is killed by the dt factor. Replacing h=1/t by exp(1/t) makes the horizontal derivative essential while preserving every relative and canonical identity; the second pair of sequences is correct.

The scope paragraph is necessary and correct. This is an obstruction to deducing a marked affine comparison from invariant form/cohomology data. It is not a counterexample to an additional theorem about the difference of two sections that both extend holomorphically into one fixed cap, and it does not identify this auxiliary Tate-product family with the six-sphere family.

## Detailed record: B_forms_translation_report.md

# B.4.29: what invariant forms do not control

## Proven scope

The proper Tate family is squared over one disc and its unique conifold is replaced by the explicit proper small resolution. Both charts have smooth total space and reduced normal-crossing centre. Ordinary fibres are products of elliptic curves, with rank-two nilpotent monodromy. Translation by exp(2πi/t) in the first factor preserves every invariant relative one- and two-form and the full integral Gauss–Manin variation. The absolute volume dt∧dlogZ∧dlogW also extends without zeros and is unchanged.

The zero section has two distinct central limits after translation, along t=1/j and t=1/(j+1/2). A hypothetical meromorphic total map would give a proper graph; its pullback to the zero section has a unique dominating curve, whose normalization is finite of degree one over the disc. It would extend the translated section holomorphically, a contradiction. Replacing 1/t by exp(1/t) makes the extra horizontal differential essential and retains the same failure.

## Source and validation

Kajiwara, Kato and Nakayama (2008), Logarithmic abelian varieties, Part I, Sections 1.1.1–1.1.3, printed pp.73–74, supplies the proper analytic Tate model. The resolution, limits, graph restriction and differential calculations are proved explicitly here. The primary article was opened and is stored outside the deliverable in external_sources/E_completion. code/e_push_forms_translation.py and its output/JSON record the exact chart and form checks. Root and boundary_d independently read and passed the full mathematical source. All missing LaTeX spacing-command backslashes identified by the root referee were corrected before integration.

## Exact remaining issue

The example changes the affine marking of one existing cap. It does not concern a difference between two sections that both extend holomorphically into that same cap: a common essential translation cancels in such a difference. Consequently the possible two-section logarithmic extension theorem remains a distinct question and is not refuted by this proposition. No conclusion about the existence of the actual six-sphere origin cap is inferred from this example alone.

## Detailed record: E_B_fullrank_completion_audit.md

# Full-rank monodromy and arbitrary analytic caps: source and bridge audit

Owner: classification_e. 15 November 2026. Uninput main-question attempt. B.4.24, B.4.26 and B.4.27 are separately proved at their stated scopes; this note does not enlarge those scopes.

## 1. What is now known

The actual Jacobian has an admissible, nondegenerate logarithmic pairing and proper analytic group models. The actual axial affine torsor nevertheless cannot extend locally trivially in that fixed log-torus category, even after finite coordinate roots and any finite sequence of point blowups over the origin. The decisive exceptional valuation block is diag(12ma,12ma+12nb), with a,b positive.

An arbitrary cap with a holomorphic fixed-bidisc map is subject to the independent finite-multisection argument: every component of its origin fibre must be a threefold. That statement does not require the cap itself to be a log torsor.

## 2. Why flattening does not finish the argument

A proper cap can be flattened after a base modification. Its resulting flat map admits local finite multisections. These local multisections are not automatically one finite multisection over a neighbourhood of the entire exceptional curve. Nor do their traces automatically extend as log-torus sections across the newly introduced exceptional divisor: the geometry over that divisor is the unknown cap, rather than either of the two prescribed fan models used in B.4.26.

This is the exact difference from the proved trace argument. There, every point of each punctured axis lies in a known fan model, and the pulled-back characters are explicitly t^k times units. On the exceptional divisor of an arbitrary flattened cap there is no established toric chart or multiplicative-character extension. Replacing this missing fact by the existence of the abstract Jacobian log torus would be circular.

The remaining possible bridges would be a theorem making every such full-rank two-torus cap a logarithmic torsor after modification, or a direct extension theorem for the difference of local multisection traces. Neither has been proved here. A graph closure of a punctured comparison is not assumed analytic or proper.

## 3. Primary-source scopes checked

1. Kajiwara, Kato, and Nakayama, *Logarithmic Abelian Varieties, Part I: Complex Analytic Theory*, J. Math. Sci. Univ. Tokyo15(2008),69–193, original PDF https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms150104.pdf. Theorems5.2.1 and5.3.2 construct proper models from a specified log torus. They do not classify arbitrary analytic completions. Example3.9.3 gives an unpolarized torus variation without a log extension, but its pairing is inadmissible; that example cannot obstruct the actual admissible pairing proved here.

2. Kajiwara, Kato, and Nakayama, *Analytic Log Picard Varieties*, Nagoya Math. J.191(2008),149–180, DOI10.1017/S0027763000025940, original publisher PDF opened. Definition7.1 imposes log de Rham comparison and Hodge goodness; Section8 additionally assumes a degree-one invariant-cycle isomorphism. Section9 supplies projective/log-Kähler sufficient conditions. These are not consequences of arbitrary properness with non-Kähler vertical components. Exact pages are recorded in B_log_torsor_references.bib and E_B_general_completion_attempt.md.

3. Kamenova, *Degenerations of 2-dimensional tori*, original author manuscript https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~kamenova/homepage_files/degen.pdf. Theorem3.1 retains both a Kähler total space and trivial canonical bundle. Its rational-component/maximal-monodromy case therefore supplies no classification at the present unrestricted hypotheses. The original source was previously opened and its access metadata is retained in B_analytic_comparison_references.bib.

4. Nishiguchi, *Degeneration of K3 surfaces, II*, original MPI preprint88-48, https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/3390/1/preprint_1988_48.pdf, reopened for this audit. The title and introduction explicitly concern K3 surfaces. The source treats non-Kähler components and presents Conjectures1.9 and4.7 about the corresponding monodromy. It is not a proved full-rank TWO-TORUS comparison theorem. It is retained as a warning against silently transferring Kähler classification, not as an authority for the missing torus bridge.

5. Nakayama's original published elliptic extension criterion, Theorem7.1.3 on p.604 of PRIMS38(2002),451–649, has been visually read together with its flattening step on p.605. It genuinely allows arbitrary proper elliptic extensions, but its relative dimension is one. The actual two-parameter period matrix admits no analytic family of elliptic quotients on any open set, even after finite base change, as proved directly in E_B_general_completion_attempt.md. Thus that reduction is unavailable.

Broad searches also returned secondary and AI-generated summaries related to complex structures on spheres. Those were not used as authorities. Search metadata is in evidence/E_B_fullrank_search_log.json. The search outcome is not a claim that no relevant theorem exists; it records that none with the required hypotheses was found or verified in this attempt.

## Detailed record: D_vertical_kato_base_attempts.md

# Changed-base vertical Kato cap: exact constraints and remaining comparison

Owner: boundary_d. Started 2026-11-15 after checkpoint08 and B.4.26.

The purpose is to test a genuinely vertical cap for the original map `(q,r)`, rather than to repeat the excluded fixed-q Kato plumbing. Near a vertical component one may have `q=r^a h(s)` with `a>0`, where `s` is a direction on an exceptional base curve. The ratio q/r^a is in general meromorphic and its poles lie on further conductor components. It must not be assumed to be a global holomorphic function on a compact component.

## An elementary constraint on a smooth Kato bundle over P1

Suppose a proposed compact vertical threefold has a holomorphic submersion to P1 whose fibres are parabolic Kato surfaces S_n, and suppose their persistent elliptic curves form a smooth relative divisor `E -> P1`. Its normal line in the threefold restricts on each elliptic fibre with degree `-n`, where `n>0`.

Then the elliptic family `E -> P1` is holomorphically a product. Here is a proof that does not assume an origin in advance.

The j-invariant is a holomorphic map from compact P1 to the affine j-line, hence constant. The Jacobian family is therefore isotrivial. Its residual linear automorphism local system is trivial because P1 is simply connected, so the Jacobian is the constant elliptic curve E0. The genus-one family is a torsor with class xi in H1(P1, E0). The exponential sequence of the constant elliptic group,

`0 -> Lambda -> O_P1 -> E0 -> 0`,

gives `H1(P1,E0) = H2(P1,Lambda) = Lambda`, because H1 and H2 of O_P1 vanish. In particular the torsor group has no torsion.

For a torsor of class xi, its relative degree-d Picard space is a torsor of class d xi, with an immaterial sign depending on the translation convention. This follows directly by trivializing over small discs: a change of origin by t translates degree-d divisor classes by d t. The actual normal line of degree `-n` gives a section of that relative Picard torsor. Hence `n xi=0`, and consequently xi=0. An origin section therefore exists and the elliptic family is the product.

The same conclusion follows topologically from the Serre differential of the fibre orientation class: a nonzero-degree line on a principal elliptic bundle forces its Chern vector to be torsion, and H2(P1,Lambda) is free. For n=1 the inverse normal line determines the origin point through the degree-one Picard identification.

**Established consequence:** a simple smooth S_n bundle over the exceptional P1 cannot use its persistent elliptic divisor to carry a nonzero elliptic Chern vector. A singular elliptic divisor, singular fibres of the threefold-to-P1 map, further conductor attachments, or a different vertical component remain possible.

## Exact period data under a changed base

On the actual cusp, the second multiplicative period has the factor `q r^12` times a nonzero holomorphic unit. Substitution `q=r^a s` therefore changes this factor to

`r^(a+12) s` times a nonzero holomorphic unit.

The first degenerating period has the factor `q=r^a s`. Thus along a transversal to a generic exceptional-base point the two Tate valuations are `a` and `a+12`, both positive. This is a maximal rank-two torus degeneration, not a family with only one torus direction degenerating. An internal Kato parameter cannot simply be set equal to s while claiming the original punctured comparison: the actual elliptic Tate multiplier still has the additional r^a factor.

The nonzero exponent `a+12` by itself is **not yet** the Chern class of an elliptic divisor over the exceptional P1. To turn the preceding product constraint into an obstruction one must construct the actual north/south overlap of that divisor from the two-period quotient, retaining the affine origin and all conductor identifications. This comparison has not been supplied. No numerical Chern class is assigned here.

## Why the earlier persistence obstruction does not settle this model

In a genuinely vertical component, every compact rational curve maps to original q=0. The derivative of `(q,r)` along its ordinary deformation space can fail to have rank two. The normal-bundle argument in B.4.23 consequently cannot be used to force that curve into a nearby ordinary q-fibre. Its stated base-map hypothesis was essential.

Likewise B.4.25 concerns a proper family of unblown Hopf surfaces with one multiplier tending to zero and the other to a nonzero contraction. It does not exclude a vertical threefold whose internal fibres have a different relation to the original base, nor does it exclude further conductor components that change those surface fibres.

## Current gap

No compact vertical threefold with the required exact two-period boundary comparison has been constructed. The missing task is the explicit global collar comparison, including the second period and the map to the original bidisc. Normal-line matching, the scalar substitution q=r^a s, and an abstract Kato bundle over P1 do not provide it.

## A different Hopf quotient that is not excluded by B.4.25

Write the two Jacobian multipliers on the dense multiplicative torus as

`g1=(q A, eta^(-1))`, `g2=(eta^6, q r^12 D)`,

where eta=exp(2 pi i mu) and A,D are units. Quotienting first by the combined period g1 g2 gives diagonal Hopf multipliers

`(q A eta^6, q r^12 D eta^(-1))`.

Both vanish along q=r^k s, of orders k and k+12. (The earlier informal message interchanged the two harmless unit factors; this display is their exact product.) Thus this combined-period Hopf quotient lies in a two-eigenvalue degeneration regime and is not ruled out by B.4.25.

It is not a cap. The remaining independent period acts on that compact Hopf surface and need not act properly there. Its two elliptic axes are generally not isomorphic before degeneration, so identifying them as conductor curves is not justified. Completing the entire period action in a toric group model would fall within the logarithmic obstruction already proved. A non-group compactification still needs an explicit, proper two-period gluing.


---

## Detailed record: ab_push_marked_graph_scope.md

# Marked-base extension and the remaining axial comparison gap

Status: a re-audit of the proved B.4.4, with an elementary consequence for the location of nonflat fibres. This note makes no new assertion that an arbitrary completion is the prescribed axial model.

## 1. What B.4.4 proves for every r-only semistable filling

Let g: Z -> Delta_r be proper, with smooth four-dimensional total space and reduced normal-crossings centre, and suppose its punctured restriction is the actual six-sphere family after any finite parameter cover. The canonical formula audited in R1 gives K_X^(-2) = f^*O_P1(1) and h0 = 2 on every nonzero fibre. Coherent proper direct image of the relative bi-anticanonical line is torsion-free on the disc, hence locally free of rank two. A frame evaluates to two holomorphic sections on Z. It is not necessary to assume cohomology base change at the centre.

The resulting meromorphic pencil is the exact fibrewise complete bi-anticanonical pencil, up to a projective coordinate change. Its explicit analytic graph is locally the incidence hypersurface va-ub=0 after removal of common divisorial factors. Proper image of the graph's critical locus is an analytic subset of P1 times the disc. The three punctured critical values are intrinsically distinguished by fibre multiplicities 3 and 4 and by the reduced cusp fibre. The horizontal critical-value curve is finite over the disc. Each labelled branch has degree one, so each extends holomorphically through the centre. The cross-ratio normalizing these three branches consequently has meromorphic coefficients. Composing it with the pencil gives exactly the original marked f over the whole punctured disc and extends meromorphically over Z.

Thus B.4.4 is valid for every finite parameter cover, and does not require an extending affine origin or an extending comparison to an auxiliary torus model. This audit found no gap in that argument or in the previous E_B44_referee.md.

## 2. Resolving the graph gives a holomorphic base map

Resolve the reduced graph by a proper analytic resolution that is an isomorphism over its smooth punctured part. The complex-analytic existence and the fact that the modification is supported over the singular locus are supplied by Bierstone--Milman, Theorem 1.6 and the paragraph following it; local finiteness is enough, and properness over a smaller closed disc makes only finitely many operations relevant. The resulting smooth fourfold Z_tilde has a proper holomorphic map

    (f_tilde,r): Z_tilde -> P1 x Delta_r,

whose restriction over r != 0 is the exact marked original family. The composed map to the r-disc is flat: its local rings are domains and r is not identically zero, so they are torsion-free over the one-dimensional regular local base. The compactness/properness statement follows by composing the proper graph projection and the proper resolution with g; the graph of the holomorphic f_tilde in this proper family is closed.

The reduced normal-crossings property of the original r-centre is not automatically preserved. A blow-up of an intersection of central branches introduces an exceptional component with the sum of their multiplicities. One may resolve the total central support, but that alone gives normal crossings with possible multiplicities. No assertion of an unchanged semistable central fibre is made here.

## 3. The base nonflat locus is finite and central

The central divisor of Z_tilde has finitely many irreducible three-dimensional components, because it is compact. Each has either all of P1 or one point as its proper f_tilde-image. Away from the finitely many point images, every fibre of (f_tilde,r) has local dimension exactly two. Indeed every fibre has dimension at least two; a fibre germ of dimension at least three would contain a three-dimensional component of the Cartier divisor r=0, and that component would map to a single value of f_tilde.

The local flatness assertion can be checked by the regular sequence (r, f_tilde-a). The smooth local ring is Cohen--Macaulay; after dividing by the nonzero divisor r its associated primes are exactly the central components. At a fibre avoiding their constant f_tilde-values, f_tilde-a avoids these primes. The two base coordinates therefore form a regular sequence and the analytic miracle-flatness criterion applies. Over r != 0, the prescribed elliptic-base map has pure two-dimensional fibres and the same criterion applies.

In particular, after shrinking a cusp disc around f_tilde=infinity, the map is flat away from the bidisc origin: all other constant component values can be excluded. The standard analytic flattening operation can thus be taken over the origin. Its strict transform preserves the punctured family and is proper and flat over the modified base. A subsequent resolution may destroy this two-dimensional flatness, so it must not be silently interchanged with flattening in a proof that needs both properties.

## 4. What the reduction does not preserve

An arbitrary r-only filling is given only over r != 0. It has no prescribed r=0 model at regular values of f. Neither the coherent pencil argument nor the graph construction identifies its central rank-one two-torus degeneration with the actual partial filling of B.4.19. Graph resolution can additionally modify that axis, including at regular q values. The construction does preserve the actual q=0 cusp family over r != 0, because that lies in the unchanged punctured r-family.

The current two-section/logarithmic bridge, as stated by classification_e on 15 November 2026, uses the explicit B.4.19 completion on both punctured axes to extend finite traces into the same associated log-torus sheaf. New exceptional base divisors over the origin have full-rank monodromy; the strict r-axis has only rank-one monodromy and a surviving elliptic kernel. The full-rank argument therefore does not settle that strict axis.

Consequently an obstruction to completing the fixed B.4.19 partial family would not yet be an obstruction to every r-only semistable filling. The remaining implication requires a theorem controlling the rank-one axial completion, or a replacement obstruction independent of its choice. No arbitrary bimeromorphic comparison, extension of affine origins, or preservation of the specified axial fan model is inferred here.

## 5. Primary-source access and scope

- B.4.4 and E_B44_referee.md were read in full again. The original Grauert/direct-image and constant-h0 base-change sources are the already opened references used there.
- Bierstone and Milman, original author preprint https://arxiv.org/pdf/alg-geom/9508005, Theorem 1.6 and the following noncompact-analytic paragraph, were independently reopened. The existing key is BM95AB. The theorem covers reduced complex analytic spaces and permits a locally finite resolution. It is used for graph resolution, not for a comparison with a different completion.
- Nakayama's original author manuscript https://www.kurims.kyoto-u.ac.jp/preprint/file/RIMS1322.pdf, Section 7.1, Step 3, printed manuscript p.141, was reopened to verify the exact use of analytic flattening of an arbitrary proper extension. His elliptic-fibration classification is not imported for two-dimensional tori.
- The original Hironaka 1975 flattening article was searched at DOI 10.2307/2373721; the direct JSTOR access failed. Its metadata is American Journal of Mathematics 97, 503--547. No assertion that the inaccessible scan was read is made. Flattening's standard role is recorded here, while the actual preservation and scope arguments above are explicit.

This is an audit note, not a newly numbered result or an unrestricted nonexistence theorem.

## Detailed record: B_two_section_bridge_working.md

# Working proof: two extending sections at a maximal torus degeneration

Status: uninput working proof, 15 November 2026. No numbered result or CSV is promoted by this file. The one-parameter argument has independent preliminary review by boundary_d. The uniform argument and final trace construction require full-source review. This concerns completion of the fixed partial family B.4.19; it does not yet exclude arbitrary r-only fillings whose r=0 axial completion differs.

## 1. Exact primary input and the erratum

Illusie, L., Kato, K., & Nakayama, C. (2005). Quasi-unipotent logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert correspondences. Journal of Mathematical Sciences, the University of Tokyo, 12, 1–66. Original publisher PDF: https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms120101.pdf . Opened and read Theorem 6.2 on printed p.32 and Theorem 6.3 on p.34. For a proper separated log-smooth analytic morphism, with log-smooth base or exact morphism, and torsion-free cokernels of characteristic groups, Theorem 6.3 gives locally free ordinary relative log-de Rham cohomology and its identification, with Gauss–Manin connection, with the nilpotent logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert extension of log Betti cohomology. No projective or Kähler hypothesis occurs. The constant sheaf is the coefficient system used here.

Illusie, L., Kato, K., & Nakayama, C. (2007). Erratum to “Quasi-unipotent logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert correspondences.” Journal of Mathematical Sciences, the University of Tokyo, 14, 113–116. Original publisher PDF: https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms140105.pdf . Opened in full. It repairs the proof of the algebraic Hodge-to-de-Rham statement Theorem 7.1, not Theorem 6.3. This proof does not use Theorem 7.1 or general analytic Hodge degeneration.

Kajiwara, Kato and Nakayama, Analytic log Picard varieties (2008), Definition 7.1(i), pp.164–165, and §9.2, p.173, explicitly make the same distinction: the comparison condition follows from IKN6.3 without projectivity, whereas their further Hodge conditions have additional hypotheses. Full PDFs are saved outside the deliverable under external_sources/E_completion/IKN2005LogRH.pdf and IKN2007Erratum.pdf; searchable text files accompany them. Web opens: turn639view0, turn639view1; exact theorem reads: turn640view0 and turn641view0/1. These identifiers are an audit trail, not substitutes for bibliographic citations.

## 2. The semistable setup, including a transverse parameter

Let f:Z→Δ_t×V be proper, with connected fibres, where V is a small disc (or a point). Assume Z is smooth and the map is semistable relative to V. Thus at every central point there are coordinates

    (x_1,...,x_k,z_1,...,z_{3-k},u),   t=x_1...x_k,   u=u,

with k≤3 and u the coordinate of V; suppress u when V is a point. The noncentral fibres are two-dimensional complex tori, viewed as torsors under a specified Jacobian family. Its integral H_1 lattice is Λ of rank four; the monodromy T=1+N has N²=0 and rank N=2. Suppose

    ker(N:Λ→Λ)=W=Zw_1⊕Zw_2

is the primitive cocharacter lattice used in a multiplicative presentation of the specified Jacobian. Its normalized invariant forms ω_1,ω_2 have integrals 2πi δ_ij on w_j. Suppose their span is a holomorphic rank-two subbundle F_A of the canonical nilpotent extension H of H¹ over Δ×V.

For the actual family, these hypotheses hold at every generic exceptional divisor of a point-blown-up base: q=t^a times a unit and r=t^b times a unit, with a,b>0. The two diagonal valuation entries are positive, and the kernel is exactly Zw⊕Zδ. In the actual period matrix, subtracting the logarithmic N-columns leaves the bounded holomorphic unit periods. The w,δ columns remain the identity, so the normalized form span F_A is indeed a subbundle. No assertion of full log-Hodge goodness of an unknown cap is needed.

### 2.1 Saturation supplies the normalized logarithmic forms

Write K•=Ω•_(Z/(Δ×V))(log), and H=R¹f_*K•. IKN6.3 applies to this semistable morphism: the characteristic map sends 1 to (1,...,1), whose cokernel is torsion-free. It identifies H with the canonical nilpotent extension of the known ordinary H¹ local system and makes H locally free. The log Betti system restricts to the ordinary system and is locally constant on the real oriented blowup of the base, so this is the same extension as that used to define F_A.

Set F=ker(f_*Ω¹_log→f_*Ω²_log). This is coherent because the displayed differential is O_base-linear between coherent direct images. The triangle K^{≥1}→K•→O_Z and its low-degree direct-image exact sequence give

    0 → F → H → R¹f_*O_Z.

The connecting map f_*O_Z→F is zero because f_*O_Z=O_base and the differential is relative. This argument does not invoke degeneration of the Hodge spectral sequence.

The quotient H/F has no t-torsion. Indeed D=f^{-1}(t=0) is reduced and each of its compact connected fibres over V has only constant holomorphic functions. Consequently f_*O_D=O_V. Apply f_* to

    0→O_Z --t--> O_Z → O_D →0.

The map O_base→O_V is surjective, so multiplication by t is injective on R¹f_*O_Z. Thus F is t-saturated in H. The specified F_A is also t-saturated, since H/F_A is locally free. Both have the same restriction after inverting t: holomorphic one-forms on a compact torus are closed and are exactly the invariant one-forms. Hence

    F = H ∩ F_A[t^{-1}] = F_A.

This equality holds with the transverse parameter u present. In particular the normalized ω_1,ω_2 are actual closed relative logarithmic forms on Z, not merely meromorphic cohomology classes. No base change theorem for H¹(O) at the centre, and no Kähler property of a component, is being assumed.

### 2.2 Local meridians have integral normalized periods

In a semistable chart, a small fibre retracts to (S¹)^{k−1}. Its angular generators are invariant under the local monodromy; the inclusion into the global fibre intertwines monodromy. Their images in Λ therefore belong to ker N=W. It follows that every local angular period of ω_j/(2πi) is an integer. These periods are constant in t and u: they pair the fixed integral class in W with the normalized forms. This is where maximal rank is essential. If rank N=1, ker N has rank three and can contain an additional elliptic period whose normalized value is not an integer.

### 2.3 Convergent relative logarithmic primitives

A closed relative logarithmic one-form on the chart has the form

    ω = Σ_(i<k) a_i(x,z,u) dlog x_i + Σ b_l(x,z,u) dz_l,

modulo dlog t. First integrate the ordinary z directions by the usual holomorphic homotopy formula. The remaining coefficients depend on x and u. Expand them as convergent series in x. For a monomial x^α, its relative weight is

    (α_1−α_k,...,α_(k−1)−α_k).

Closedness says that the coefficient vector of this monomial is proportional to that integer weight. If the weight is nonzero, divide by one nonzero weight entry to obtain a holomorphic primitive for that monomial. The denominator has absolute value at least one, so the primitive series converges on every sufficiently smaller polydisc. If the weight is zero, α_1=...=α_k and the monomial is t^n. These resonant terms give base-dependent dlog coefficients. Their angular periods are exactly those coefficients; since the periods of ω are constant integers, all nonconstant resonant coefficients vanish and the constant coefficients are integers.

Thus, after shrinking the chart,

    ω_j = d_rel h_j + Σ_(i<k) n_ij dlog x_i,    n_ij∈Z,

with h_j holomorphic in all variables, including u. The exponentiated primitive

    χ_j = exp(h_j) ∏_(i<k) x_i^{n_ij}

is a genuine meromorphic function whose divisor is supported on the central coordinate branches. Its logarithmic differential is ω_j relatively. Negative integer exponents are allowed: these are log characters, not necessarily holomorphic functions on the whole chart.

### 2.4 Difference of two extending sections

Let a,b be holomorphic sections of f over Δ×V. Fix u_0. The central fibre is compact and connected. Choose finitely many semistable charts whose central intersections form a chain joining a(0,u_0) to b(0,u_0). Shrink V so the same chain works for every u. Every nonempty open intersection with the reduced NC fibre contains a smooth central point. At such a point f is a submersion; choose a holomorphic transverse auxiliary section in the intersection. Thus the endpoints and all auxiliary sections are holomorphic, with no new root required at this stage.

Within one chart, evaluating χ_j at its two sections and taking the ratio gives a monomial in t times a holomorphic unit. Each section avoids all central coordinate branches when t≠0; consequently the pullback of each x_i is t^m times a unit, with an integer m≥0. Multiplying these character ratios along the finite chart chain gives a pair of log characters. On each ordinary fibre this pair is the exponential of the integral of (ω_1,ω_2) along a path from a to b, so its class modulo the specified period lattice is exactly b−a in the Jacobian. Changing the path adds an integral period. Since the pairing has full positive rank along t=0, every integral ghost vector is bounded in the KKN sense. Therefore b−a extends as a section of the specified associated logarithmic torus A.

This is a relative statement in u, obtained from holomorphic chart functions and holomorphic auxiliary sections. It does not follow merely by testing separate transverse discs. It also explains why B.4.29 is not a counterexample: in that example the reference origin does not extend under the new affine marking; the difference of two genuinely extending sections is insensitive to their common essential translation.

## 3. Obtaining this setup generically along an exceptional curve

Suppose a proper analytic completion of the fixed partial family B.4.19 exists over the bidisc. Flatten its map by a modification of the base supported at the origin. Resolve the modified surface base and dominate its modification by a finite sequence of point blowups. Flatness is preserved by further base change. Retain the strict transform of the component dominating the base. The resulting map has relative dimension two; the exceptional base locus is a compact tree of rational curves.

At the generic part of one exceptional curve E, resolve the total space and the inverse image of E, preserving the ordinary torus locus. There are finitely many components and strata over the compact exceptional set. Delete from E the images of the critical loci of the strata maps to E and any nondominating strata. These are discrete analytic sets, hence finite on the compact curve. Around any remaining point, the total divisor and the transverse parameter u have local equations

    t = unit·x_1^{m_1}...x_k^{m_k},    u=u,    k≤3.

The unit can be absorbed in one local coordinate after taking a root locally. Choose a common multiple M of the finitely many m_i. After t=τ^M and normalization, the local model is toroidal, with integral height-one vertices. A compatible triangulation of the height-one lattice polygons, using all their lattice points, has unimodular triangles: a lattice triangle with no extra boundary or interior lattice point has area 1/2. The subdivisions agree on shared edges after inserting all edge lattice points. They give a proper smooth toric modification with reduced normal-crossing τ-fibre. These operations are uniform in u and are unchanged on τ≠0. No projective or Kähler assumption on the original cap is used; only analytic resolution and these finite local toric modifications are used. The polynomial local toric modifications glue because their ideals are monomials in the globally marked divisor branches; a common refinement handles overlaps. This paragraph needs final referee attention to its compatibility and source scope.

Local finite multisections of the flat map split into honest holomorphic sections after an additional finite cyclic root at a generic point of E. A proper modification has a meromorphic lift of each such section; its indeterminacy on the two-dimensional section base is isolated, so deleting finitely many further points of E makes the lift holomorphic. Equivalently, resolve the finite section surface and restrict away from the exceptional images. Only finitely many multisections and exceptional curves are used below, so a single finite bound on all required root degrees is available after stratifying those curves.

On this semistable cover, §2 applies to any two resulting extending sections. Therefore the difference of the two traces extends as an A-section after the cyclic root. Taking its multiplicative norm gives a positive integer multiple of the difference on the original base. Explicitly, for a character τ^k u(τ,u_0) on a cover t=τ^e, its norm is

    ∏_(j=0)^(e−1) (ζ_e^j τ)^k u(ζ_e^j τ,u_0)
      = (−1)^{k(e−1)} t^k · unit.

Changing a character lift by a period changes the norm by an integral period; the periods themselves descend from the original base. Thus the norm gives an actual A-section downstairs and equals e times the original ordinary Jacobian section. At a generic exceptional divisor all character ghosts are bounded. This norm argument is on ordinary analytic sites and does not presume Kummer descent for the quotient sheaf.

## 4. Hartogs for sections of A, with the Y ambiguity retained

Let p be a point of the smooth modified base and take a sufficiently small bidisc V_p about p, with its normal-crossing boundary. Put V_p^*=V_p\{p}. The period lattice sheaf Y=Z² is constant here, and H¹(V_p^*,Y)=0 because the punctured bidisc retracts to S³. The usual-topology exact sequence

    0→Y→T_log^(Y)→A→0

therefore lifts every A-section on V_p^* to a section of T_log^(Y).

B.4.27 identifies the exact bounded sheaf on the modified base as M_(D_Q+E)^gp × M_(D_Q+D_R+E)^gp. For either lifted character, its divisor order along each boundary branch is a fixed integer. Divide by the corresponding local boundary monomial. The result and its inverse are holomorphic on the punctured bidisc, so ordinary Hartogs extends both. Their product remains one. Restoring the monomial gives an extending bounded character; the forbidden strict-r order of the first character stays zero. Hence the A-section extends across p.

The extension is unique. Locally lift the difference of two extensions to T_log^(Y). On the punctured connected bidisc this lift is an integral Y-period, locally constant and therefore constant. Subtract that period and use the identity theorem for its two characters. Thus the A-section is zero on the whole bidisc. This uniqueness also preserves all cocycle identities across the missing points. The result concerns A-sections; H¹ of A on a punctured bidisc can be nonzero and torsors need not extend.

## 5. Gluing multiplied trace origins and the fixed-partial-family contradiction

Choose a finite open cover U_i of a neighbourhood of the compact exceptional tree in the modified base S. Flatness supplies on each U_i a finite multisection V_i→U_i of positive degree d_i, obtained by a transverse local analytic slice and a finite representative. Let d be a common positive multiple of the finitely many d_i. On U_i minus the exceptional tree, the trace of V_i, multiplied by d/d_i, is a section t_i of the d-fold contracted product P^d of the actual punctured A-torsor P. Along the strict transforms of the two original punctured axes, the prescribed fan models make this trace a genuine log section by the character-norm proof of B.4.26.

On an overlap, t_i−t_j is an ordinary A-section away from the exceptional tree. By §3, a positive integer multiple of every such difference extends across the generic part of every exceptional curve. Choose one common positive multiplier m for the finite collection of curves, multisections and local ramification types. Replace all t_i by m t_i; now they are sections of P^(dm). Their differences extend across every exceptional curve apart from finitely many points. Section 4 extends them across all those points, including crossings. Uniqueness makes the cocycle equations hold everywhere.

Glue trivial A-torsors on U_i, using these extended differences, to P^(dm) off the exceptional tree using the trace identifications. This constructs an A-torsor on S extending P^(dm). B.4.27 gives H¹(S,A)=0, so its restriction must be trivial. But B.4.24 proves every positive multiple of the actual punctured torsor nontrivial: its r-loop connecting class is dm·γ, which cannot be split by the two extending axial origins. This is a contradiction.

If all preceding generic-reduction and uniformity steps pass, the conclusion is: the particular actual partial filling B.4.19 has no proper analytic completion over its missing origin, even allowing a purely three-dimensional origin fibre and modifications of the base. No Kähler or projective hypothesis has entered.

## 6. Scope which must not be silently enlarged

An arbitrary smooth r-semistable filling of the original six-sphere family has a meromorphic extension of the intrinsically marked f by B.4.4. Resolving its graph makes the fixed (q,r) map holomorphic, but can replace the r=0,q≠0 axial completion. Along that strict r-axis the linear monodromy has rank one. Its kernel contains an extra elliptic period, so §2.2 does not force integral residues there. The existing Hopf insertions are compatible with this distinction.

Accordingly the final contradiction currently uses the prescribed B.4.19 fan model on that axis. It cannot yet be stated as nonexistence of every r-only filling. To reach that stronger result one must either compare arbitrary r-axis caps to the prescribed log origin, or replace the fixed-A trace construction by a category controlling the extra invariant elliptic direction. This is an actual remaining gap, not a consequence of the one-parameter lemma or Hartogs.

## 7. Current audit and next checks

- IKN2005 Theorem6.3 opened directly; 2007 erratum read and scoped.
- Low-degree saturation and the local integer-weight primitive have independent preliminary D review.
- Parent independently proposed the multiplied-trace/Hartogs construction; the full written proof awaits review.
- Check generic analytic resolution/semistable reduction uniformly in u, including gluing the toric subdivisions and a common finite norm multiplier.
- Obtain a directly opened primary flattening theorem statement, in addition to Nakayama's published use of analytic flattening in Theorem7.1.3 Step3.
- Do not update a CSV, input, or global nonexistence claim until these checks are complete.

### Further primary audit before checkpoint09

Enokizono and Hashizume, *Semistable reduction for complex analytic spaces*, arXiv:2404.05124v3 (12 October 2024), original https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.05124, was opened. Lemma2.14 on manuscript pp.6–7 proves the correspondence between proper toroidal analytic modifications and subdivisions; that lemma has no projectivity hypothesis and supports gluing the local lattice subdivisions in §3. Their full Theorem1.1 does require projectivity and is expressly not used. The introductory sentence credits one-dimensional analytic semistable reduction to KKMS; the local surface-fibre construction above is retained explicitly rather than importing the higher-dimensional projective theorem.

The original Hironaka1975 JSTOR article, DOI10.2307/2373721, was searched and its stable and PDF links were opened, but they returned an error/empty wrapper. It has not been read directly. Nakayama's original published Theorem7.1.3 Step3 already read at p.605 uses analytic flattening without adding a projectivity assumption to the putative extension. A directly readable primary flattening statement is still being sought.
